<a id="r2"></a>
# Reference R2: Parts, roles and contracts

*Ratchet: closed-loop security validation for AI-assisted engineering. Reference R2. Edition 5.2.1, Thesis 1.4.3, 2026-10-31, build 2b8e8a14+local.*

Every part with its technology class (open-source examples are illustrations, never requirements), quality tier, what it does when something it depends on fails, and whether one product can replace another behind a stable contract (swap point) or an existing tool usually serves it first.

<a id="r2-systems"></a>
## Systems

| Code | System | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| S1 | Context and intent | Permission-scoped facts; owned ISA rules; freshness, completeness and unknowns. |
| S2 | Validation workflow | Requests, durable tasks, producers and scoped artifacts. Typed proposals only. |
| S3 | Authorized effects | Exact policy/approval/grants, runner enforcement and bounded execution cells. |
| S4 | Model/data gateway | Approved local/hosted routes; qualification and untrusted output handling. |
| S5 | Proof and evidence | Protected fixtures/oracles, independent checks and finding dispositions. |
| S6 | Hardening and regression | Owner-reviewed fixes, deployed retest and change-triggered recurrence checks. |
| S7 | Evaluation and operations | Matched replay, whole-loop economics, reliability and independent challenges. |
| shared:EXT | Existing enterprise systems and authority | Existing estate, engineering workflows, SOC and approved third-party endpoints that the validation systems reuse rather than replace. |
| shared:PEOPLE | Accountable people and roles | Engineering, security, IAM, SOC and risk: define scope, approve effects, accept evidence. |

<a id="r2-trust-zones"></a>
## Trust zones

| Zone ID | Zone | What it means |
|---|---|---|
| `reasoning` | Reasoning and workflow boundary | S1/S2 workers + S4 gateway. No estate secrets; scoped context/artifacts. Target content remains untrusted. |
| `control` | Authorization and proof boundary | S3 policy/lease authority + S5 oracle/evidence writes + S7 acceptance. Separate permissions and accountable administration. |
| `execution` | Bounded execution cells | Isolated source/build, representative staging and approved production cells; no worker-to-target bypass. (View E, C0 Execution and integration) |
| `evaluation` | Protected evaluation records | S7 frozen cohorts, held-out partitions, evaluation artifacts and qualification records. Producers cannot read held-out partitions or write records; no execution authority (replays go through S3). |
| `shared:existing` | Existing enterprise operations | CI/releases retain change control; SOC preserves independent observations. Model provider egress occurs only through S4 eligible routes. |
| `shared:third-party` | Third-party inference boundary | Conditional access to contracted hosted frontier endpoints. |

<a id="r2-failure-behaviors"></a>
## Failure behaviors

| Behavior | What it means |
|---|---|
| fail closed | The request is denied or the action does not start. No effect occurs. Callers see an explicit denial. |
| queue and retry | Work waits durably and resumes on recovery. Nothing is lost; elapsed time grows. |
| mark unknown | Missing or stale data is labeled unknown, never current. Consumers that need it refuse to proceed. |
| degrade to manual | The step falls back to the recorded manual procedure for its fidelity rung. |
| continue without | The loop continues; the gap is recorded (telemetry, sampling). Never used for a control. |
| posture from policy | Behavior when unavailable is the pre-decided, signed posture from s3.gate-failure-policy. |

<a id="r2-parts-by-plane"></a>
## Parts by plane

### Context plane (9 parts)

Facts, intent, consequence and snapshots the reasoning side may read.

| Code | Part | What it does | Technology class | Open-source examples | Trust zone | Tier | When a dependency fails | Flags | Core or module |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| `s1.identity-validity-resolver` | <a id="r2-part-s1-identity-validity-resolver"></a>Identity and validity resolver **(enforces a rule)** | Typed joins, source precedence, freshness and conflicts. Critical facts get a live recheck at S3. | Entity resolution service | Splink, Zingg | reasoning | Orchestration | mark unknown: unresolved identity is unknown |  | [core: context plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-context) |
| `s1.context-compiler` | <a id="r2-part-s1-context-compiler"></a>Scoped context compiler **(enforces a rule)** | Access policy before joins and caches; task-sized views. Return incomplete/unknown states explicitly. Labels call sites that are covered-by-road for a class (from the road conformance check), so producers can spend their analysis on business logic and off-road paths; the label narrows priority, never permission or coverage claims, and sampled covered call sites are still sent for testing. Passes leads to producers as structured, schema-bound fields only (tool, tool version, rule id, location, confidence, timestamps, quarantined classification); submitter- and tool-written free text never enters producer context. Computes each context item's outsider label set from its source records, with no model call; a producer never declares one. Passes dispositions from the evidence store as identifiers, statuses, enumerated fields and references only. | Snapshot build job (deterministic, content-addressed output) | Dagster, Apache Airflow | reasoning | Orchestration | mark unknown: deterministic output per input versions; incomplete snapshot refuses decision-critical runs |  | [core: context plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-context) |

| `s1.criterion-registry` | <a id="r2-part-s1-criterion-registry"></a>Intent and criterion registry **(enforces a rule)** | Owner-approved ISA/invariant versions, scope, exceptions and expiry; references to protected oracles only (location and hash). | `s1.normative-store` | <a id="r2-part-s1-normative-store"></a>Approved normative store **(enforces a rule)** | Rule/oracle refs; expiring feedback; private answers excluded from snapshots. | `s1.snapshot-index` | <a id="r2-part-s1-snapshot-index"></a>Snapshot and dependency index | Snapshot references, data class, query completion and invalidation links; expiring attributed feedback. Partitioned like source facts. Serves the live precondition resolver by prefetch with signed expiry, never synchronously at decision time. | Content-addressed artifact store | OCI registry via ORAS and Harbor, MinIO | reasoning | Orchestration | queue and retry: content-addressed, immutable entries |  | [core: context plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-context) |
| `s1.consequence-register` | <a id="r2-part-s1-consequence-register"></a>Consequence register (crown jewels and mission impact) **(enforces a rule)** | Versioned consequence facts. Per asset: a required tier (crown-jewel \| critical \| standard) and an optional numeric impact_score (null by default, supplied by an organization profile when it has a scoring method). Per service: the cost of blocking a deploy (mission or life-safety criticality, also tiered). Low-toil attestation: the testing team drafts each tier from material the organization already holds (business impact analysis and continuity tiers, data classification, service catalog criticality), and the business owner confirms or corrects it. Every fact records who drafted it, from which sources, who confirmed it and when. Re-attestation is triggered by material change through the dependency-scoped invalidation rule; a calendar maximum is a profile option. A fact with no confirmation is unattested and is read as the organization's chosen unattested_default; the unattested count is reported as a register-gap metric. Solvers receive only scoped labels through the context compiler, never the register, because the full register is itself a target map. Model output never writes here. | Versioned relational register with attestation and expiry | PostgreSQL with temporal tables, Backstage software catalog for ownership | control | Evidence integrity | mark unknown: versioned attestations with drafter and confirmer; unconfirmed reads as unattested, which gate policy maps to the profile's unattested_default | existing tool first | [core: context plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-context) |

Every lead is labeled with its provenance and a lead status. Agent discovery runs (scope-only agents that people point at repositories or infrastructure) are a lead source like any tool: their findings are valid locally and unproven end to end until a scenario's proof condition is met. | Relational lead store with provenance, separate from the fact store | PostgreSQL, DefectDojo as existing-tool rung | reasoning | Orchestration | queue and retry: leads append with versions; status changes only from accepted evidence or sampled outcomes | existing tool first | [module: Existing security tools as leads](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-tool-portfolio) |

Requests, durable workflow, dispatch and producers. Proposes; never executes.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Scenario intake (step 1) accepts a scenario made by hand, from a lead, or from the organization's existing discovery practice (people running agents against repositories or infrastructure). Each scenario records its owner, its originating finding or lead with provenance, where its owner-signed proof condition lives, and where the end-to-end check runs (approved test environment, replica, read-only production, or the production-effects module) or that it settles by review. | Intake and status UI or CLI | Backstage plugin | reasoning | Best effort and analytics | degrade to manual: status reads may lag | existing tool first | [core: orchestration plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-orchestration) |

Runs with no tools, no artifact write path and no producer context: it sees one lead's free text and returns a schema-bound classification (failure class from an enumeration, location reference, rule reference, confidence band, bounded-length summary flagged untrusted). Its output is validated against that schema before it is stored beside the lead; it never reaches a producer as free text and never sets a lead's status. Its model requests pass the request-data gate like any other. | Quarantined, tool-less model worker (dual-LLM pattern) with schema-constrained output | worker behind the AI gateway with no tool bindings, Pydantic output validation | reasoning | Best effort and analytics | continue without: free text waits unread; structured lead fields still flow; output rejected unless it validates against the classification schema |  | [module: Existing security tools as leads](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-tool-portfolio) |
| `s2.typed-decision-worker` | <a id="r2-part-s2-typed-decision-worker"></a>Typed decision worker | Answers declared decision types with a typed model route: lead triage and deduplication, the probability behind the developer-queue confidence bar, scenario ordering inside a consequence tier, consequence-tier drafts for the testing team, gate-health anomaly scores, and output flags that send an agent result to human review. Calls models only through the request-data gate, only on routes the registry marks qualified for that decision type, and with structured inputs (no secrets, no proof conditions). Each decision type declares a fallback (frontier text route or a rule) used when the typed route is down, stale or narrowed. Emits TypedDecision records, which are advisory: they never authorize, never count as coverage, never set a verdict, never close a finding and never attest a tier (lint rule model-advisory). Exploitability is never a decision type. | Typed decision worker calling typed model routes through the AI gateway, with a declared fallback per decision type | worker behind the AI gateway with typed-output routes, scikit-learn classifier as the baseline arm | reasoning | Best effort and analytics | continue without: each decision type drops to its declared fallback (frontier text route or a rule); nothing waits on a typed route | swap point | [core: orchestration plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-orchestration) |
| `s2.proof-condition-drafter` | <a id="r2-part-s2-proof-condition-drafter"></a>Proof-condition drafter | Drafts a scenario's proof condition for its owner: what the running system must show for the weakness to count as real, what would show it is not, and what must still work for legitimate users. Reads the finding or lead and the scenario's scope, which may carry outsider-written text, so it runs like the quarantined lead reader: no tools, no artifact write path, no context store shared with test-drafting agents, and its model requests pass the request-data gate. Its draft goes only to the scenario owner, who corrects it and signs it into the fixture and oracle registry; an unsigned draft is never a proof condition and never reaches a test-drafting agent. | Tool-less drafting model worker on its own route, output shown only to a person | worker behind the AI gateway with no tool bindings and no shared memory store | reasoning | Best effort and analytics | continue without: the scenario owner writes the proof condition without a draft; nothing else waits |  | [core: orchestration plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-orchestration) |

### Model access plane (6 parts)

The single path to models, with data gates and route qualification.

| Code | Part | What it does | Technology class | Open-source examples | Trust zone | Tier | When a dependency fails | Flags | Core or module |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `s4.model-data-gateway` | <a id="r2-part-s4-model-data-gateway"></a>Model/data gateway **(enforces a rule)** | Receive a proposed ModelRequest bound to task/data/tool/context/fixture eligibility; return typed untrusted output and an effective-route/usage receipt. Before it serves a self-hosted route, checks the digest of the weights loaded on the endpoint against the digest the route registry pins; on a mismatch it takes the route out of service, reports it to the registry and routes down the degradation ladder (F103). | AI gateway (single egress to models) | LiteLLM proxy, Envoy AI Gateway | reasoning | Orchestration | fail closed: no route, no request; never falls back to an unlisted route | swap point, existing tool first | [core: model access plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-model-access) |
| `s4.route-budget-broker` | <a id="r2-part-s4-route-budget-broker"></a>Route and budget broker **(enforces a rule)** | Choose qualified local/hosted configuration; scoped provider credentials, rate limits and cost budgets; approved fallback only. Walks the degradation ladder: frontier route, then self-hosted open-weight route, then the scripted no-agent arm, then manual. Never routes a producer below the lowest rung it declares, never to an unqualified or stale route (last failover drill older than the dial interval), and when no rung is available pauses the producer and queues its work for the manual rung. Every rung change is recorded with its cause (outage, policy or access loss, budget, stale fallback). | Route, fallback and budget broker | LiteLLM router and budgets | reasoning | Orchestration | fail closed: budget unknown means request refused |  | [core: model access plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-model-access) |
| `s4.qualification-route-registry` | <a id="r2-part-s4-qualification-route-registry"></a>Qualification and route registry **(enforces a rule)** | Versioned record of every model route: provider, model and version, hosting (self-hosted or hosted), output kind (text or typed), the data classes it may receive, its rung on the degradation ladder and its last drill date. A typed route also lists the decision types it is qualified for, each with the reliability curve and calibration error from its qualification run; it is eligible only for those types. A self-hosted route is pinned to the digest of the weights released for it. The registry pins a released digest only when the release attestation that comes with it verifies, as the local enforcement point verifies software and images before they start: the release signature against a current key reference whose purpose is release that key management sends to the registry, and, under that signature, provenance naming the organization's build platform at SLSA Build Level 3, an attested digest equal to the released one, and an artifact name and version equal to the route's model and version. A release that fails pins nothing: the registry records the refusal with the check that failed, sends it to protected audit, and the route keeps its last verified version. A pinned digest is a new route version that stays unqualified, and serves nothing, until promotion review requalifies it, and a digest mismatch the gateway reports marks the route unqualified. Route entries change only through promotion review, a verified release and a digest mismatch the gateway reports. | Versioned route and model registry with an attestation check before each pin | MLflow model registry, Git-backed registry, Sigstore cosign for the attestation check | reasoning | Evidence integrity | fail closed: unqualified route refused |  | [core: model access plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-model-access) |
| `s4.inference-endpoints` | <a id="r2-part-s4-inference-endpoints"></a>Inference endpoint adapters | Self-hosted open-weight serving or a contracted hosted frontier endpoint; governed data retention, region and subprocessors. The self-hosted route is the fallback rung and is sized to run the failover drill and every producer whose lowest rung is self-hosted; its capacity is a profile field. | Model serving (self-hosted open-weight or hosted) | vLLM, Text Generation Inference, Ollama | reasoning | Best effort and analytics | queue and retry: provider outage pauses runs; fallback only to an eligible route | swap point, existing tool first | [core: model access plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-model-access) |
| `s4.route-usage-receipts` | <a id="r2-part-s4-route-usage-receipts"></a>Route and usage receipts | Effective model/version, retry/fallback, input/output references and cost; scoped retention and protected logging. | Usage telemetry store | OpenTelemetry into ClickHouse | reasoning | Best effort and analytics | continue without: usage gaps flagged in cost ledger |  | [core: model access plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-model-access) |
| `ext.hosted-frontier-endpoint` | <a id="r2-part-ext-hosted-frontier-endpoint"></a>Approved hosted frontier | Contracted model / endpoint / use case; approved data, retention, region, subprocessors. | Third-party hosted model API |  | shared:third-party |  |  | swap point, existing tool first | [core: model access plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-model-access) |

### Control plane (17 parts)

Exact-action authorization, grants, credentials, audit, gate failure policy, keys, privileged access, policy release and the model-boundary gates.

| Code | Part | What it does | Technology class | Open-source examples | Trust zone | Tier | When a dependency fails | Flags | Core or module |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `s1.live-precondition-resolver` | <a id="r2-part-s1-live-precondition-resolver"></a>Live precondition resolver **(enforces a rule)** | S1-owned interface. The S3 policy decision point calls it before every grant; unresolved facts stop or escalate the action. Returns evidence, not credentials. Runs in the control zone, so a reasoning-zone compromise cannot answer a recheck on its behalf. | Low-latency fact recheck service with short-TTL cache | service plus Valkey | control | Control safety | fail closed: fresh read at decision time; consulted by the policy decision point |  | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s3.action-control-service` | <a id="r2-part-s3-action-control-service"></a>Action control service **(enforces a rule)** | Receive an authenticated, normalized ActionSpec; issue a denial or short-lived exact ExecutionGrant; produce a runner ExecutionReceipt including actual identity/target, observations, limits and cleanup. For attack injection, refuses with no person involved any injection past the pipeline whose layer or environment the declared injection policy does not allow, and every one while no policy is declared, before an approval request exists. Issues each injection grant with its restore approved inside it and an expiry at the service owner's time limit; a production grant also expires within the exposure window the profile sets, and is issued only for attack injection the approved production profile names explicitly. With each injection grant it issues a signed, single-use restore grant with the same expiry, sealing into it the restore credential the lease broker issued for that grant, and delivers it to the local enforcement point of the cell that applies the injection; the injection does not start until that cell holds it. The restore at expiry is the cell's to run, with no call to this service or the lease broker, and this service only records it. On the trusted agent's stop it revokes the grant at once and tells the cell to run the held restore now, within the organization's ceiling from stop to the environment restored. Failing closed covers effects not yet started: while this service or the lease broker is down no new injection starts, and every running one is still restored by its cell at the time limit. | Authorization front door (external authorization API) | Envoy ext_authz service | control | Control safety | fail closed: denies when any dependency is unavailable; failing closed covers effects not yet started, and a running attack injection is restored by its cell at the time limit with no call to this service |  | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s3.action-broker` | <a id="r2-part-s3-action-broker"></a>Action broker **(enforces a rule)** | Validate primitive/schema; normalize actual targets and destination rules. Reject changed or unknown requests. | ActionSpec normalizer and broker | service behind Envoy | control | Control safety | fail closed: canonical normalization by a versioned normalizer per reviewed action primitive, the same normalizer the bounded runner uses to parse what it executes; hash over normalized ActionSpec; an action whose normalizer version differs from the runner's is refused |  | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s3.policy-decision-point` | <a id="r2-part-s3-policy-decision-point"></a>Policy decision point **(enforces a rule)** | Resolve current facts, required human approval, data/environment scope and exact effect envelope. Required human approval is tiered by consequence: an action outside its producer's design limit, or one whose effects reach an asset at or above the organization's consequence line, needs a person's approval of that exact action; an action inside the limit and below the line may run on a reviewed grant alone (a standing permission or batch approval) once the trust gate holds. With no limits declared, approval is exactly as before limits existed: kept regression tests replay on their reviewed per-batch approvals and standing permissions along the cadence ladder, and every other action needs a person's approval. Limits declared while the consequence line is unset change nothing yet: no new grant-only action runs until the line is set. Once limits and the line are set, a producer with no limit has every action approved by a person, and kept-test replays sit inside their producer's limit like any grant. Honors a grant only when it was checked against the current limit version. Reads limits from its signed, versioned copy of the dial register's limits and current tiers from its copy of the consequence register at every decision, so neither register's outage stops a decision; a narrowing or a tier rise is pushed at once and applies on arrival, and while either copy's lease has lapsed every action that would run on a grant alone goes to a person (F104); an unattested asset counts at the profile's unattested default. | Policy engine | Open Policy Agent, Cedar, Cerbos | control | Control safety | fail closed: default deny; decisions logged before effect | swap point | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s3.approval-service` | <a id="r2-part-s3-approval-service"></a>Approval service **(enforces a rule)** | Authorized person / role / workflow; reason, expiry, exact action binding. Issues three kinds of approval to match the cadence ladder: per run (periodic), per batch naming tests, targets and window (scheduled), and standing permission for one reviewed test, target, identity and environment with an expiry (continuous). With no design limits declared these serve kept regression replays, as before; once limits and the consequence line are set, batch approvals and standing permissions may also serve other reviewed agent actions inside their producer's limit. Standing permissions are revoked when the planner steps a test down. On each new limit version, re-checks every outstanding batch approval and standing permission against it and revokes any that is wider. Reads limits from its signed, versioned copy; while that copy's lease has lapsed it issues no new batch approval or standing permission and sends each request to a person action by action (F104). Seeded known-bad proposals from the sampling service arrive at the dial-register rate (default 1 in 50 approvals), indistinguishable at the console; their outcomes give approval, edit and reject rates a denominator. Never issues a standing permission or batch approval wider than the design limit of the producer it serves, or reaching an asset at or above the consequence line; such a request goes to a person action by action. Counts the approval requests sent to each approver and holds them to the dial register's rate per approver per hour and to the approver's work-in-progress limit; requests past either wait in order, so no approver receives a flood, and a count past its trailing baseline alerts the control-plane operator and the seeding owner as a possible attack on the gate. While the rate is unset, the work-in-progress limit alone bounds the requests and the gap is reported. | Approval workflow bound to action hashes | approval workflow on Temporal, Flowable | control | Control safety | fail closed: no approval means no grant | existing tool first | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s3.grant-approval-store` | <a id="r2-part-s3-grant-approval-store"></a>Grant and approval store **(enforces a rule)** | Authoritative policy versions, bindings, approvals and leases. Agents cannot mint or rewrite them. | Strongly consistent grant store with atomic single-use consume | PostgreSQL serializable transactions, etcd | control | Control safety | fail closed: linearizable single-use consume; expiry checked against a trusted clock with skew margin |  | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s3.protected-audit` | <a id="r2-part-s3-protected-audit"></a>Protected audit service **(enforces a rule)** | Correlate intent, denial, grant, action, outcome and cleanup; audit failure blocks controlled high-risk effects. Append-only and tamper-evident (hash chain) with WORM retention whose custody sits with the independent assurance owner: control-plane operators can read it and can never alter or delete it. Records every administrative session and command from the privileged access broker before the command runs, every key event, and every weights release the route registry refuses, with the check that failed. | Append-only, tamper-evident audit log | Trillian, immudb, Kafka to object-locked storage | control | Control safety | fail closed: audit write acknowledged before effect; audit unavailable stops high-risk execution | existing tool first | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s3.credential-lease-broker` | <a id="r2-part-s3-credential-lease-broker"></a>Credential / lease broker **(enforces a rule)** | Scoped, short-lived test identity; secrets delivered only to runner. For an exposed secret's status probe it releases the sealed old credential to the runner for that one probe series only, and asks key management to destroy it after the series' last attempt. With each injection grant it issues one restore credential to the action control service: a lease scoped to the restore's target and effect, sealed to the cell that applies the injection and valid until the grant's expiry plus the cell's clock-skew margin, so the restore never asks it for a lease at run time. | Secrets and workload identity broker issuing short-lived scoped credentials | OpenBao, SPIFFE/SPIRE | control | Control safety | fail closed: no lease, no credential; leases expire independently of the caller | swap point, existing tool first | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s4.request-data-gate` | <a id="r2-part-s4-request-data-gate"></a>Request and data gate **(enforces a rule)** | Minimize/classify payload; enforce legal-entity and task/data eligibility before routing; block unauthorized export. Eligibility is the first decision: which sources and data classes a task may send to which route, from the organization's data classification and the route's eligibility. Content detection (secrets, personal data) is a second layer behind it, because detectors recognize those far better than source code, designs or business logic. Load-bearing: for every producer route that holds untrusted content, non-public data and an outbound path, this gate is one of the controls on the exfiltration leg, beside the quarantine that keeps outsider text from producers and the route registry that admits only eligible endpoints; its payload scoping is tested on every change (F39) and no producer reaches a model endpoint around it. A model-boundary control: it sits inline on the model path but is deployed and administered in the control zone, apart from the gateway it guards; its decision is deterministic (no model call), its policy arrives only through the policy release pipeline, and it sends nothing into the authorization plane. Computes each model request's outsider label set as the union of the labels on the context items the run was given and on everything the run has received since (earlier responses, and the observations its receipts record, a target's response counting as outsider text), and records it under the attempt id, so the response and everything the run produces inherit it. A label set a producer supplies is ignored and logged. | Outbound data classification and redaction filter | Microsoft Presidio, Open Policy Agent | control | Control safety | fail closed: payload not classified means payload not sent |  | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s4.response-proposal-gate` | <a id="r2-part-s4-response-proposal-gate"></a>Response and proposal gate **(enforces a rule)** | Schema-check output; treat content as untrusted. Emit findings/actions/patch proposals, never execution authority. Schema validity does not certify a judgment: content influence remains open, so dispositions are decided in S5. A model-boundary control: deployed and administered in the control zone, apart from the gateway; deterministic validation only, and its output goes to producers and usage receipts, never to an authorization or execution part. | Response schema validator (output treated as untrusted data) | JSON Schema validation with Ajv, Guardrails AI | control | Control safety | fail closed: invalid schema is rejected; output never grants authority |  | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s3.approval-console` | <a id="r2-part-s3-approval-console"></a>Exact-action approval console **(enforces a rule)** | Authenticated approvers see the exact action rendered by the console from the action broker's normalized ActionSpec, the record the authorization service decides on: its primitive, target, effect envelope and prerequisite evidence. No text an agent or producer wrote is shown as the action; a proposer's rationale, where shown, sits apart, marked as the proposer's words with its outsider label. Approvers approve or deny with reason and expiry bound to that exact action. Each approver also sees the outcome of every run they approved: its receipt summary and its disposition, as the independent reviewer recorded it. Records approval-rate, edit and latency telemetry per approver and action class, and the approval-rate trend per approver and action class against a trailing baseline; drift past the dial-register threshold first checks whether proposal quality changed, then opens a recalibration task, and rotation is the last step, each recorded with its outcome. Seeded known-bad proposals measure gate health; each approver's own catch rate goes to that approver and to the seeding owner, never to a manager, and seeds are not treated as a control that keeps approvers vigilant. | Approver UI outside producer networks and identities | Backstage plugin on a separate identity realm | control | Control safety | fail closed: unavailable console means no approvals, not implied approvals | existing tool first | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s3.gate-failure-policy` | <a id="r2-part-s3-gate-failure-policy"></a>Gate failure policy **(enforces a rule)** | For each rejecting gate (regression stage, replay class), the failure posture to apply when the validation platform cannot answer: fail-closed, fail-open-with-record, or fail-open-under-expiring-acceptance. Computed when the finding is accepted, from the consequence tier of the assets its demonstrated path reaches (recorded at disposition; composed by the path-impact graph module where enabled), the blocking cost of the protected service, and threat signals. Keys on tier today. Core defaults: fail-closed when the path reaches a crown-jewel or critical asset, fail-open-with-record for standard; profiles may override with attribution. Unattested assets take the profile's required unattested_default. The schema accepts an impact_score key once a profile supplies scores. The posture applies only to deploys that touch the regression's declared dependencies (artifact, policy, identity), reusing the dependency-scoped invalidation rule; while the gate is unavailable every other deploy proceeds and is recorded. Thresholds are set by the security risk owner through the dial register. Ship-through on a critical or crown-jewel path is an attributed, short-lived acceptance by the business risk owner with security risk owner concurrence (signers changeable by profile). The posture is signed by key management with an expiry from the posture_expiry_hours dial (default above the worst credible recovery time, including region loss) and shipped with the pipeline stage, together with the tier defaults from the profile, so it applies when the control plane is down. An expired or untrusted posture falls back to its tier default (crown-jewel and critical fail closed; standard fails open with a record), never to fail-closed for a standard path, so an outage's blast radius does not grow with its length; an alert fires when any posture nears expiry. Every fail-open event owes an automatic replay once the platform recovers. Ship-through is reported as a rate per path, team and tier beside the deploy-gate SLO, and a path reaching the renewal cap (default 2 consecutive acceptances) refuses another until its posture is reviewed. Each path's posture review reads gate outcomes (outage holds, ship-throughs, post-recovery replay results, deploys held without a regression failure) and is triggered by the renewal cap, a failed post-recovery replay or the profile's interval. | Signed policy registry with offline-verifiable postures | Open Policy Agent bundles signed with cosign, The Update Framework (TUF) | control | Evidence integrity | fail closed: signed postures with expiry from the posture_expiry_hours dial; consumers verify offline; an expired or untrusted posture falls back to its tier default held offline in the stage, never to fail-closed for a standard path |  | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s7.dial-register` | <a id="r2-part-s7-dial-register"></a>Dial register **(enforces a rule)** | One versioned register of every dial Ratchet can turn: cadence step per regression, tier posture thresholds, ship-through renewal cap and posture review interval, developer-queue confidence bar, sample rates per frame, seeded-proposal rate, approver and verifier WIP and queue-age limits, handoff ceiling per claim, regression time budget per service, and the misses floor of each derived check, which the security risk owner sets. Each dial carries its floor or ceiling, current setting, safe direction, who may turn it and the evidence for the last turn; every turn appends with actor and evidence. A turn past a floor is refused unless it links a risk acceptance by the security risk owner, and the evaluation report shows each dial's history. Consumers read signed settings; while the register is unavailable no dial can be turned and consumers keep the last signed setting. Design limits and the consequence line are the exception: each version is signed with an increasing number and a lease, a narrowing is pushed to every reader at once, and a reader whose lease lapses sends grant-only actions to a person (F104). In S7 beside promotion and qualification records, in the control zone so no producer can write it. | Versioned settings register with signed changes and an approval workflow | Git with branch protection and signed commits, Open Policy Agent bundles | control | Evidence integrity | fail closed: append-only history; while unavailable no dial can be turned and consumers keep the last signed setting; design limits and the consequence line are read from a versioned copy under a lease, and a reader whose lease lapses sends grant-only actions to a person | existing tool first | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s3.privileged-access-broker` | <a id="r2-part-s3-privileged-access-broker"></a>Privileged access broker **(enforces a rule)** | The only path to administer any part in the control zone. Just-in-time, time-bound sessions under dual control: the requester plus a second approver who is not the requester; break-glass additionally needs the security risk owner. Standing administrative credentials do not exist. Every session and command is recorded to protected audit before it runs, under custody the operator cannot alter. Direct database, console or key access to any control-zone part outside this broker is denied by network policy and alerts (F55). A session never edits evidence, grants, learned items, design limits or the consequence line: those change only through the part that owns them, signed. The broker itself is administered only through break-glass. Every AdminSessionRequest and AdminSessionApproval, including refused inputs that issue no session, is durably appended to protected audit before the broker acts. The record binds the authenticated sender, request reference, payload hash, scope, time, decision and refusal reason. Requester and approver cannot alter the record. An audit failure refuses the input and opens no session. Retries append attempts without overwriting earlier decisions. | Just-in-time privileged access broker with dual control and session recording | Teleport Community Edition, JumpServer | control | Control safety | fail closed: broker down means no administrative access, never direct access; break-glass needs dual control and the security risk owner | swap point, existing tool first | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s3.key-management` | <a id="r2-part-s3-key-management"></a>Key management **(enforces a rule)** | HSM- or KMS-backed signing for every offline guarantee: gate failure postures, replay window bundles, injection restore grants, credential leases, execution receipts, dial settings and design limit versions, sample frames, context snapshots, evidence partitions, policy bundles, Ratchet's own software, image and weights releases, per-cell rollback authorizations and release-state provisioning with purpose release, learned items, fix briefs and secret revocation records. Private keys never leave it; consumers verify with versioned key references. Keys rotate on a schedule with overlapping validity windows: the new key signs while the old key still verifies until everything it signed has expired or been re-signed (F58). A compromised key is revoked, everything it signed is re-signed or marked untrusted, and consumers treat an untrusted posture as expired (F59). A restore grant the cell verified and took in before its key was revoked still runs at expiry, because undoing the injection is the safe direction; one that arrives after the revocation is refused. Keys are separate per evidence partition. | HSM- or KMS-backed signing and key service with versioned key references | OpenBao transit engine, SoftHSM via PKCS#11, Sigstore cosign for artifacts | control | Control safety | fail closed: no signature, no new posture, bundle, lease or receipt; consumers keep verifying with cached key references until verifies-until | swap point, existing tool first | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s3.policy-release-pipeline` | <a id="r2-part-s3-policy-release-pipeline"></a>Policy release pipeline **(enforces a rule)** | The only path by which policy, egress allowlists, route tables, gate configuration and store schemas change, and the only path by which Ratchet's own software, runner and cell images and self-hosted model weights reach any part. Changes are code: the existing negative tests run as policy unit tests, a deny-diff report shows what a change would newly allow, and any widening needs the security risk owner's approval; rollout is staged (canary cell, then teams) with automatic rollback on a failing check or error-budget burn; schema changes to append-only stores use expand-and-contract migrations so a rollback never strands data (F56, F57). Software and images are built on a hosted, isolated build platform that generates and signs SLSA Build Level 3 provenance, follow the organization's secure development requirements, and are released with a signature from key management. Self-hosted weights are ingested on the same platform, whose provenance records the file's upstream source and digest (it attests the ingest, never how the model was trained), and are released by digest with a signature from key management; the route registry pins the digest as a new, unqualified route version once the attestation verifies (F102, F103, F131). While Ratchet's own error budget is burned, two kinds of change still release: a change whose deny-diff newly allows nothing (a narrowed limit, a revoked policy, a removed allowlist entry), and a security fix to Ratchet's own parts. Each is recorded with its direction and still passes the deny-diff, the policy unit tests and the staged rollout, and a security fix whose deny-diff widens anything still needs the security risk owner's approval. Every other change waits for the budget to recover or for an exception the security risk owner records. Withdrawal is per cell in this edition: a rolled-away release is refused on the stable logical cell that rolled back; another cell can still admit it. Fleet withdrawal needs a separate protocol in the next minor edition. It durably assigns every release a new increasing sequence and every cell rollback a new increasing rollback number, including automatic rollbacks. Key management signs releases, rollback authorizations and provisioning state with purpose release. The rollback authorization travels in the release attestation and binds the cell, expected highest verified sequence, running sequence being left, older target sequence and digest, rollback number and issued-through release sequence. It records every issued release and rollback in protected audit, reconciles each accepted older start with exactly one issued rollback number, and never reissues a rolled-away release sequence. To return to code at or above a rolled-away-from sequence it builds a fresh release with a new sequence above the cell's high-water mark and refusal ranges. It maintains the durable per-cell provisioning record outside disposable cells, including the mark, consumed rollback number and accumulated refusal ranges; authenticated provisioning supplies a new or rebuilt cell's first state from that record and refuses a stale or unavailable record, including after a storage restore. | Policy-as-code pipeline with unit tests, deny-diff, staged rollout and expand-and-contract migrations | Open Policy Agent test with Conftest, Argo Rollouts, Liquibase | control | Evidence integrity | fail closed: pipeline down means no release; the current signed policy keeps running; rollback is itself a release | existing tool first | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |
| `s6.fix-cap-gate` | <a id="r2-part-s6-fix-cap-gate"></a>Fix-cap gate **(enforces a rule)** | Applies each owning team's fix cap: the number of open validated findings the team accepts at once, set by the team with the security role that agreed it, or Ratchet's default cap when the team set none. It is the team WIP cap the saturation signal and the dispatcher read. The gate checks the cap when a finding is validated, before the security side prepares its fix path, so no fix path is prepared for a finding the cap would refuse, and checks it again when the complete fix path arrives, at queue entry. A validated finding whose demonstrated path reaches an asset at or above the organization's consequence line passes the cap: it enters the queue and is recorded in the fix-cap refusal log as over the cap. So does a finding that a sourced exploitation signal matches by rule (a known exploited catalog listing or observed exploitation, on its vulnerability identifier or on the component and version its weakness sits in), which also starts the organization's exploited window, from the later of validation and the signal's date; so does a finding with an external disclosure deadline; and so does a refused finding the security risk owner sends after two consecutive held reviews. Any other finding that would take the team above its cap is refused into the log with its reason and the cap in force; it stays accepted in the finding ledger and counts in every measure. While the organization has set no consequence line, no finding passes the cap on consequence; while neither the public known exploited catalog nor an observed exploitation source is wired, none passes on exploitation; and while the organization sets no exploited window, an exploited finding passes with no window and the gap is reported. Inside a consequence tier, findings waiting for room under a cap enter in order: exploited first, then by the entry point's exposure (reachable from the internet first), then automatable first, then by age. An exploitation signal, exposure and automatability never lower a posture, move a finding to a lower tier or count as evidence. Only the gate writes refusals and over-cap records; the security side's producers and the owning team cannot write, change or remove one. It decides by rule, with no model call. While the gate is unavailable, a validated finding whose disposition records a pass ground (an asset at or above the consequence line, an exploitation match or an external deadline) goes to its owning team without the gate, and every other validated finding waits with the security side with its wait measured; none is dropped. When the gate returns, it writes an over-cap record for each finding that entered while it was down, with the pass ground it entered on, after checking that ground again against its own inputs; a finding whose ground does not hold on that check goes to the security risk owner, and the records join the next refusal review. | Rule-based admission check over the findings pipeline, reading signed cap values and the consequence line | Open Policy Agent, a policy rule in the tracker's automation run under the control-zone identity | control | Orchestration | fail closed: unavailable means only a finding whose disposition records a pass ground (at or above the consequence line, exploited, or under an external deadline) enters an owning team's queue, and the gate writes its over-cap record and checks the ground on recovery; every other validated finding waits with the security side, its wait measured; none is dropped; only the gate writes refusals | swap point | [core: control plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-control) |

### Execution plane (8 parts)

Isolated cells where granted actions run.

| Code | Part | What it does | Technology class | Open-source examples | Trust zone | Tier | When a dependency fails | Flags | Core or module |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `s3.local-enforcement-point` | <a id="r2-part-s3-local-enforcement-point"></a>Local enforcement point **(enforces a rule)** | Verify/consume applicable approvals immediately before effect; enforce expiry, destination, time, health and replay rules. For an attack injection, holds the signed restore grant that came with the injection grant, with the restore credential sealed inside it, verified offline against cached key references, and refuses to apply an injection whose restore grant it does not hold or cannot verify. A dead-man timer on the cell's trusted time source consumes the restore grant and has the runner run the restore under the restore credential at the grant's expiry, with no control-plane call and no lease request, or earlier when the action control service relays a stop. Running the restore is idempotent, so a restore the service owner's rollback already made leaves nothing to undo. Each restore is written to the local audit buffer before it runs and reported to the action control service afterwards. At deploy, starts a runner, cell image or Ratchet software release only when its release attestation verifies: signature against a current key reference whose purpose is release, SLSA Build Level 3 provenance from the organization's build platform, and the running digest equal to the attested one; anything else is refused, recorded and alerted, and the cell keeps its last verified release (F102). Each release attestation carries a monotonically increasing release sequence number covered by the release signature. Release signatures and rollback authorizations verify only against a current key reference whose purpose is release. For release admission a cell is a stable logical execution destination, independent of its disposable image or enforcement process. Withdrawal is per cell in this edition: a rolled-away release is refused on the stable logical cell that rolled back; another cell can still admit it. Fleet withdrawal needs a separate protocol in the next minor edition. The local enforcement point keeps that cell's highest verified release sequence, last consumed rollback number and sticky refusal ranges in durable storage outside the disposable cell; all survive restart, rebuild and restore. A new or rebuilt cell takes its first mark and rollback state from the release pipeline through authenticated provisioning, never from the first arriving release or an empty local store; unavailable or stale initialization refuses all starts. A rollback authorization is part of the release attestation and is signed by key management for the pipeline with purpose release; it names the cell, its highest verified sequence, the running sequence being rolled away from, the older target sequence and digest, a per-cell rollback number and the highest release sequence issued before this rollback. The enforcement point requires the target to be older than the running release and the rollback number to exceed the last consumed number, and atomically persists its consumption and the new refusal range before starting the target. A rollback never lowers the high-water mark and permanently refuses every previously issued release from its rolled-away-from sequence through its signed issued-through sequence, even with another rollback authorization. These refusal ranges accumulate; reusing that code at or above a rolled-away-from sequence requires the pipeline to issue a fresh release with a new sequence beyond the applicable refusal ranges and the high-water mark. Outside these ranges an older release still requires a matching unused rollback authorization; a missing, invalid, reused or non-increasing authorization is refused before any start. It refuses, records and alerts on every failed admission, including reused authorizations and sticky-range replays. Its protected audit event names the cell, prior and resulting release sequences and rollback number, so every older start matches exactly one rollback event on the pipeline's own release record. | Local egress and action enforcement (sidecar or network policy) | Envoy sidecar, Cilium network policy | execution | Control safety | fail closed: destination allowlist bound to grant; enforcement local to the cell; a hostname is resolved at execution and the resolved address is checked against the grant, so an approval binds the address actually reached; refuses an injection whose signed restore grant it does not hold, and its dead-man timer runs that restore at the time limit with the control plane down |  | [core: execution plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-execution) |
| `s3.bounded-runner` | <a id="r2-part-s3-bounded-runner"></a>Bounded runner deployments **(enforces a rule)** | S3 local enforcement + source/staging/production profiles; scoped credential leases and outcome/cleanup receipts. | MicroVM or sandboxed runner | Firecracker, gVisor, Kata Containers | execution | Control safety | fail closed: runs only with a consumed grant; cleanup verified | swap point | [core: execution plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-execution) |
| `s3.source-build-cell` | <a id="r2-part-s3-source-build-cell"></a>Isolated source/build cell **(enforces a rule)** | Disposable, tested OS/VM boundary; protected original, scratch candidates. Controlled dependency preparation; no runtime internet. | Hermetic build sandbox with no runtime internet | BuildKit in Firecracker, Bazel remote execution | execution | Control safety | fail closed: no runtime internet |  | [core: execution plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-execution) |
| `s3.staging-cell` | <a id="r2-part-s3-staging-cell"></a>Representative staging cell **(enforces a rule)** | Test identities and synthetic resources; preserve relevant controls and document differences from production. | Isolated test environment whose targets under test run in a sandboxed runtime or behind a separate VM, cluster or account boundary | Kata Containers runtime class, gVisor runtime class, separate cluster or cloud account per cell | execution | Control safety | fail closed: isolation per team profile; a target under test runs in a sandboxed runtime or behind a separate VM, cluster or account boundary, because it receives attack payloads and a shared kernel is one container escape from other cells | existing tool first | [core: execution plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-execution) |
| `s3.production-cell` | <a id="r2-part-s3-production-cell"></a>Approved production cell **(enforces a rule)** | Specific reviewed primitives and destinations; local enforcement, leased identity, health stops, cleanup and SOC escalation. | Change-managed production action profile (reversible actions only) | same runner class with production policy profile | execution | Control safety | fail closed: health pause and stop; reversible actions only |  | [module: Production effects](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-production-effects) |
| `s3.observation-receipt` | <a id="r2-part-s3-observation-receipt"></a>Observation and receipt | Runner-side capture of correlated action outcome, policy/approval versions and telemetry, written to protected audit; the runner cannot edit prior records. S5 intake independently validates receipts before they count. Signed with a key from key management; held in the cell's outbox until S5 intake acknowledges it. After a restore, receipts, grant consumption and audit are reconciled, and any attempt whose effect cannot be matched is marked effect-uncertain. | Signed execution receipt and attestation | in-toto attestations, Sigstore | execution | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: signed; held in the outbox until S5 acknowledges; receipt loss marks the attempt effect-uncertain |  | [core: execution plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-execution) |
| `s3.local-audit-buffer` | <a id="r2-part-s3-local-audit-buffer"></a>Local audit buffer **(enforces a rule)** | Append-only, hash-chained audit buffer inside the execution cell. The runner writes the audit record here before each effect; the buffer drains to protected audit asynchronously. A buffer that cannot write, or that is full, stops execution (fail closed), so protected audit leaves the deploy-time path without weakening fail-closed audit. | Append-only local audit buffer with hash chain and forward shipping | Fluent Bit filesystem buffer, Vector disk buffer | execution | Control safety | fail closed: a buffer that cannot write or is full stops execution; drains to protected audit with the hash chain verified |  | [core: execution plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-execution) |
| `s3.replay-verdict` | <a id="r2-part-s3-replay-verdict"></a>Replay verdict check **(enforces a rule)** | For a pre-authorized regression replay, compares the observed outcome with the reviewed test's registered expected-outcome hash carried in the window bundle and returns pass, fail or unknown to the pipeline stage. Deterministic, no model. Unknown (target unavailable, cleanup unverified, hash mismatch on the bundle) applies the stage's posture. The receipt still goes to S5 intake, which can overturn a verdict on reproduction. For a finding of kind exposed secret it also judges the revocation check by the credential type's verification recipe: the issuer's status by identifier, which needs no value, where the issuer offers it; otherwise the status probe, an authentication attempt with the old credential, which counts as refused only on the codes the recipe maps to revoked or invalid and only when every attempt of the series, spread over the issuer's propagation time, is refused. It returns revoked, refused, accepted or unknown to S5 intake with each receipt, with no model; accepted or unknown leaves the finding open. The status probe is a reviewed primitive that may run from phase 2 without the production-effects module, from a fixed egress, only after the detection side has the announcement. The old credential reaches the cell only from the credential lease broker for that probe series, sealed under the owning team's key, and no model, lead or record ever holds its value. | Deterministic verdict check against registered expected-outcome hashes | in-toto verification, Open Policy Agent evaluation | execution | Control safety | fail closed: unknown applies the stage's posture; a verdict never comes from a model; hashes only the reviewed test's declared projection of the observation (refused or allowed, status class, assertion results), never timestamps, session ids or ordering, so a run that varies only in excluded fields gets the same verdict |  | [core: execution plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-execution) |

### Evidence plane (21 parts)

Intake, independent verification, disposition, ledger and evidence stores.

| Code | Part | What it does | Technology class | Open-source examples | Trust zone | Tier | When a dependency fails | Flags | Core or module |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `s5.proof-evidence-service` | <a id="r2-part-s5-proof-evidence-service"></a>Proof and evidence service **(enforces a rule)** | Receive candidate artifacts and execution observations; return a FindingDisposition with original evidence mode, tested scope/state and unresolved assumptions. | Evidence API service (sole writer to evidence stores) | service on Kubernetes | control | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: sole writer to evidence stores |  | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s5.evidence-intake-validator` | <a id="r2-part-s5-evidence-intake-validator"></a>Evidence intake validator **(enforces a rule)** | Validate source, transformation, schema, target freshness and grant/lease binding before proof. Check that every lead a candidate finding cites exists in the tool lead store; a citation is provenance only and never substitutes for reproduction. Honors a finding validated under an earned-autonomy grant only when the grant was checked against the current version of its design limit; otherwise the finding goes to independent reproduction. Attaches each candidate finding's outsider label set from the request-data gate's records for its run and ignores any label set the producer supplies; a field whose labels cannot be computed counts as labeled. | Schema and provenance validator | JSON Schema, in-toto verification | control | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: rejects incomplete receipts with a reason; holds valid ones until stores recover |  | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s5.independent-verifier` | <a id="r2-part-s5-independent-verifier"></a>Independent proof verifier **(enforces a rule)** | Protected original; valid synthetic assertions or reproduction. Relevant variants and legitimate behavior checks for repair. Carries outsider labels field by field: it clears only the fields it constrains (the verdict, status classes, assertion results, references and hashes), and steps, payloads and the target's output keep their labels in the receipts and outcomes it sends on. Attaches the outsider label set of an artifact it takes from the artifact exchange from the request-data gate's records for the run that produced it. When its reproduction stops short of the proof condition, it records where the path stopped and whether its receipt observed a control refusing the step (the part, rule or setting and its version); only an observed stop can hold a finding, and a stop it cannot attribute to a control is reported as a failed reproduction. | Independent reproduction harness on its own credential path | workflow on Temporal running pytest or Go test harnesses | control | Evidence integrity | degrade to manual: manual reproduction is the recorded fallback |  | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s5.repair-checks` | <a id="r2-part-s5-repair-checks"></a>Independent repair checks **(enforces a rule)** | Apply candidate to separate workspace; rebuild; PoV variants; legitimate behavior; do not modify the acceptance harness. | CI test execution against unmodified harness | Tekton, Jenkins | control | Evidence integrity | degrade to manual: unmodified harness checksum verified | existing tool first | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s5.disposition-review` | <a id="r2-part-s5-disposition-review"></a>Disposition and review service **(enforces a rule)** | Confirm/reject/unresolved with explicit scope. A negative or dismissive producer or model output is recorded but never counts as coverage evidence; only a passing deterministic regression or a human-accepted negative test does. Independent institutional staff accept material assurance or exceptions. When an accepted finding's weakness reached production where an attacker can reach it through the running system, writes its escape record: detection source and role, the role and method that attributed it to a deployment from the machine deployment record, or no attributable deployment when none shipped it, and whether it fell within the organization's detection window, the confirming role (never a detecting role, the producer team or Ratchet decision owner), and its cost from the assets the path reached, the closure record and the verified fix. A reproduction that failed with no observed stop is recorded unresolved, reason reproduction failed, and never rejected. Records a finding held only from an independent reproduction whose receipt shows a control refusing the path short of the proof condition, under a finding kind that declares held, and only with the id of its held-dependency test in the regression catalog; it refuses a held disposition without one, and a control nobody observed in a reproduction holds nothing. It reads each held-dependency test's status from the catalog: while the test is missing, has no owner or interval, or is past its interval, the finding reads unresolved; when the test's run meets the proof condition, it records the finding confirmed with the reach that run demonstrated and the tier recorded at hold, with no new triage and no model, and sends it on like any accepted finding. | Disposition workflow with attribution and expiry | workflow on Temporal with review UI | control | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: attributed, expiring dispositions | existing tool first | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s5.fixture-oracle-registry` | <a id="r2-part-s5-fixture-oracle-registry"></a>Fixture and oracle registry **(enforces a rule)** | Owner-controlled build/harness, expected assertions and private values; candidate agent tests need independent review.
