| `s5.protected-evidence-store` | <a id="r2-part-s5-protected-evidence-store"></a>Protected evidence store **(enforces a rule)** | Append-only or versioned raw observations, failures and cleanup; solvers cannot alter prior evidence or expected outcomes. Partitioned by jurisdiction and business unit from the profile, each partition under its own key from key management; reads across partitions only through the review console, attributed. Restore drills verify the hash chain before the store accepts writes again (F60). | Immutable object store (object lock, write-once) | MinIO object locking, Ceph RGW object lock | control | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: write-once |  | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s5.finding-ledger` | <a id="r2-part-s5-finding-ledger"></a>Root-cause and finding ledger **(enforces a rule)** | Record of decided dispositions: dedup lineage; accepted/rejected/unresolved/held records; attribution, expiry and invalidation for dismissals and risk acceptance. A held record keeps the weakness, the control the reproduction observed, the path and the id of its held-dependency test, and the conversion when one happens; an unresolved record keeps its reason. Written only by S5 disposition; no S2 or S3 write path. Partitioned by jurisdiction and business unit from the profile. Each accepted finding carries a canonical path key: its weakness class plus a defined path, made of the service and entry point the demonstrated path starts from, the identity or role it acts as, and the asset or action it reaches, each normalized to the names the scope and the consequence register use. A finding is novel when no earlier record has the same path key, and recurring when one does or when a road entry claims to retire its class; dedup lineage and the remediation measures match on this key. An accepted finding whose weakness reached production also carries its escape record: who detected it and through which source, the deployment it is attributed to (or none attributable) with the role and method that attributed it, and whether it was detected within the organization's detection window, who confirmed it (a role apart from every detecting role, the producer team and Ratchet decision owner), and its cost: the consequence tiers of the assets reached and the services affected, time to recover, and whether the fix removed the weakness class or one instance. | Relational append-only ledger | PostgreSQL append-only tables, DefectDojo as existing-tool rung | control | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: append-only; writes only through disposition | existing tool first | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s6.release-state-correlator` | <a id="r2-part-s6-release-state-correlator"></a>Release and state correlator **(enforces a rule)** | Bind approved correction to actual artifact or effective policy revision in the stated deployment; expose missing joins. Writes the deployment record into closure as a machine record (commit, build, artifact digest, deployed workload or effective policy revision, environment); a missing join leaves closure open, it never asks a human to vouch for deployment. For a finding of kind infrastructure misconfiguration it judges the closure check: it reads the live configuration state of the named resources through the estate's own interfaces, with no model, compares it with the declared intent (the policy or configuration rule and its version), and records match, mismatch or unknown; only a match closes, and a merged or applied change to the infrastructure code alone never does. Records the SLSA build level of the provenance each deployment record rests on; a closure or escape attribution resting on provenance below Build Level 2 is labeled self-attested in every record and measure, because below that level the team that ships the fix also writes the record that says it shipped. | Build and deploy provenance correlator | GUAC, SLSA provenance with in-toto | control | Evidence integrity | mark unknown: unknown deployed state cannot close |  | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s6.retest-regression-planner` | <a id="r2-part-s6-retest-regression-planner"></a>Retest and regression planner **(enforces a rule)** | Request S2/S3-approved retest with valid prerequisites; require intended defense and legitimate behavior, not incidental failure. For a constraint-enforced claim, include an adjacent-path hypothesis set and varied-path fixtures. Run each accepted regression in the cadence mode the organization chooses for it: periodic (with the existing assessment cycle, each run approved), scheduled (fixed schedule under a batch approval) or continuous (every relevant change under a standing permission). Propose a step up only when the trust gate holds: a run of clean replays with no false failures, no out-of-scope attempt, no harmful run or cleanup failure, and a stable target. Step down automatically on any of those events. For services the road conformance check passes, replace per-service regressions of retired classes with the road entry's road-level regression; reinstate them on escape-hatch use or a failed check. Every road-level replay runs under the normal S3 authorization envelope. Bounds the delivery tax: each service has a regression time budget in the dial register; a replay runs only when a change touches its declared dependencies, and replays run in parallel; regressions whose class reaches constraint-enforced closure or a vetted road entry are consolidated; stage time per service is reported as a trend against the budget. A kept test leaves the blocking path only by a recorded route and is never deleted. Demotion moves only routine runs: a test that has not failed within the organization's quiet period still runs, and still blocks, on every change that touches its declared dependencies; only its extra time-based runs move to the scheduled lane, which runs on the organization's lane interval, and a failure there still blocks the next deploy in its dependency scope. Quarantine: a test with false failures leaves the blocking path, keeps running, and counts as an open exception under the exception policy, within the organization's cap on how many and for how long; at expiry it is fixed, re-proved or retired. Retirement: proposed when a paved-road entry or a derived check covers the test's weakness class and that check is itself kept and passing; before the security risk owner may accept it, the covering check runs against this test's isolated reverted-fix copy under the normal authorization envelope and must fail there; it takes effect only when the security risk owner accepts it, and lapses back to active when the covering check fails or is withdrawn. Re-proof: on the organization's cadence each kept test, in either lane, runs against an isolated copy with its fix reverted under the normal authorization envelope and must fail; the purple team's AI may run it. A "no longer fails" result moves the test out of the blocking lane only after the verifier independently confirms the result; the test is then proposed for retirement or repair. A copy whose revert conflicts, or that no longer builds or starts, is reported not re-provable with the fix's age: never as no longer failing, and never a step toward retirement. While the organization leaves the quiet period unset, no run moves to the scheduled lane; while it leaves the quarantine limits unset, a quarantine lasts at most the exception ceiling and the count is uncapped but reported; while it leaves the re-proof cadence unset, the gap is reported and no cadence is assumed. Attack injection: plans each experiment that reintroduces a verified fix's weakness class, names the detection layer expected to catch it (workstation, pull request, pipeline, deployed test environment or production), writes its injection record, and submits every injection as an ActionSpec under the normal authorization envelope, with the restore that undoes it inside the same ActionSpec and an expiry at the service owner's time limit. At the pre-deploy layers the injected change is never merged or deployed: it runs without notice, ends when the layer catches it or passes it, and may repeat on the re-proof cadence. A pull request injection opens as a draft or on an isolated branch, carrying a required status check that fails while the injection is open, so the repository refuses any merge attempt, and it closes when its grant expires. A workstation injection runs in a harness that stamps its start and finish, never on a developer's own machine. At the pipeline layer the injection is the reverted-fix copy the re-proof runs. Routine re-proof injection runs count against the service's regression time budget and appear in its stage-time trend. Past deployment it plans an injection only into an environment the profile's injection section allows, under the notice mode that section declares, and none while the section is absent or holds a placeholder; the action control service refuses any other. In the trusted-agent mode the trusted agent can stop it at any point, and the stop goes straight to the action control service. A production injection runs only while the production-effects module is enabled, as attack injection named explicitly among the reversible effect classes its approved production profile lists, through the production cell. It also runs each class test of the secure-creation loop: a rule the policy-owning team turned from a proven class, run against the class's reproduced members at their revisions before the fix. It takes the rule and the members from the regression catalog, proposes the run as an ActionSpec like any retest, so it runs in an execution cell under the normal authorization envelope with its effects recorded, and returns the result to the catalog. The members' code and the rule's output never leave Ratchet's cells and catalog. This independent aggregate validation is the held-out layer. Its proof conditions and chained-path validations never reach a coding agent. A component-local instance test may be visible and pass while this layer finds a remaining path. That variant becomes a new finding through the normal loop and never silently changes a team pull request. There is no separate variant probe set. | Scheduler on the durable workflow engine | Temporal schedules | control | Orchestration | queue and retry: owed replays persist across outages |  | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s6.change-closure-records` | <a id="r2-part-s6-change-closure-records"></a>Change and closure records **(enforces a rule)** | Review/deploy/retest references; separate technical closure, residual risk and risk acceptance. Technical closure has two states: path broken (the demonstrated chain fails) and constraint enforced (varied paths to the same objective also fail under a held-out check). A finding closes only through the closure its kind declares in the finding-kind registry: the kind's closure check, judged with no model by the part the kind names, records the expected outcome; the kind's kept gate is in the regression catalog; nothing the kind lists as never closing it is counted as closure; and two attributed human acceptances by distinct identities (review of the correction, retest outcome). For a path finding the check is the independent retest with a machine deployment record from the release-state correlator; no human signs for what the correlator proves. Every closure carries an environment label (staging, production or another declared environment) from phase 2, and a staging closure never reads as production risk reduction. A finding of kind exposed secret closes path broken only when the revocation check records the credential failed by its type's recipe, which takes the place of the machine deployment record, with the owning team's revocation and rotation and each derived session's end recorded through its fix path, the kept scan rule that blocks the secret's return in the regression catalog, the detection side's exposure-use review finding no unexplained use, and the same two acceptances; a type with no recipe closes on the owning team's attestation and the closure is labeled attested. Removing the secret from code, history, logs or tickets is recorded and never closes it. Each closure records its kind's recovery measure: time to revoke for an exposed secret, from detection to the credential failed. | Relational closure records (append-only) | PostgreSQL | control | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: two attributed human acceptances plus a machine deployment record; environment label on every closure; append-only | existing tool first | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s6.regression-catalog` | <a id="r2-part-s6-regression-catalog"></a>Reviewed regression catalog **(enforces a rule)** | Versioned fixture/invariant/scope dependencies; material changes invalidate assurance and trigger scoped replays. Holds two scopes of regression: per-service regressions and road-level regressions that test a paved-road entry once for every service proven to use it. Holds each kept test's lane for its time-based runs and its status (blocking or scheduled; active, quarantined, retired or set aside pending repair), its last failure and last re-proof, and the record of every lane or status change with who decided it. A "no longer fails" re-proof result changes the test's status only with the verifier's independent confirmation; a not re-provable result changes nothing and is recorded with the fix's age. A retirement is recorded only with the security risk owner's acceptance, the covering check's own kept status, and the covering check's failing result against this test's reverted-fix copy; a retired test keeps its record and code so it can be reinstated. Holds held-dependency tests beside the regressions: each runs a held finding's path with the observed control in place against the weakness's proof condition, names its owner (the team that owns the control) and its re-test interval, and is run by the retest planner at least once per interval under the normal authorization envelope. It sends each held-dependency test's last run and verdict to disposition review, so a test missing, unowned or past its interval makes its held finding read unresolved and a run that meets the proof condition converts the finding. It reports, per control, how many held findings the control holds and how many of their tests have failed, so a control that holds many weaknesses is visible as the most valuable test to keep running and the share of credited controls that fail is measured. For the secure-creation loop it derives each derived check from a static or in-process kept test, and the check's pattern is general to the weakness class, never the kept test's input, fixtures or expected behavior. It keeps the full result of each class test, a policy-owning team's rule run against the class's reproduced members at their revisions before the fix, and sends agent-reachable parts only pass or fail per rule version under ClassTestCount. It sends per-class counts directly to the policy-owning team: people, never an agent under ClassTestTotals, never through the check suite, measurement plane or model route. The full class-test result and all member details stay in the catalog. It hands each class test to the retest planner, which runs it in an execution cell under the normal authorization envelope as it runs a kept test, and receives the result back; the members' code and the rule's output never leave Ratchet. It also counts, per derived check, its kept test's replay failures on changes deployed since the check's version was issued, from the replay verdicts it receives, and sends the loop only that count, never a replay result. Security owns the protected aggregate validation and the class-level paved roads and guardrails. The class-level checks remain visible to agents only as derived checks. The catalog sends metadata for every protected kept test to the kept-test mirror. The mirror cannot edit a test, its verdict, lane, retirement or owner. Team-owned component-local tests are kept in the team suite and mirrored separately. | Test repository plus catalog index | Git repository per service, Backstage catalog | control | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: versioned tests with dependency headers | existing tool first | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s5.review-console` | <a id="r2-part-s5-review-console"></a>Independent evidence review console **(enforces a rule)** | Independent staff review proof mode, scope, receipts and unresolved assumptions; record accept, reject, unresolved or risk-acceptance requests with attribution. Producer roles cannot authenticate here. The only path for reads across evidence partitions; each such read names the reviewer, the partitions and the reason, and is recorded. Clearing outsider-labeled free text is an approval action class here: a person decides each clearing, which is recorded with the fields cleared; open clearing requests count against the clearer's approver WIP limit; seeded requests carrying a planted instruction arrive at the seeded-proposal rate and are reported when cleared; and approval-rate drift and the time from request to decision are measured per clearer, as for every approval action class. | Reviewer UI outside producer identities | Backstage plugin on a separate identity realm, DefectDojo | control | Evidence integrity | degrade to manual: reviewers fall back to the recorded manual procedure | existing tool first | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s5.path-impact-graph` | <a id="r2-part-s5-path-impact-graph"></a>Demonstrated path-impact graph **(enforces a rule)** | Links each accepted finding's reproduced path to the consequence-register assets it reaches, and composes accepted findings that chain into longer demonstrated paths. Edges exist only for paths an independent verifier reproduced; hypothesized or model-inferred paths are excluded, and model exploitability judgments never set an edge or a weight. Attacker-side threat signals annotate edges (observed exploitation, known exploited lists) but cannot create one. Each demonstrated path also records the tool leads that pointed at it (lead provenance); a lead annotates a path and never creates one. Lives in S5 rather than S1 because every edge is accepted evidence, written only by disposition review, so producers cannot shape the consequence a gate will act on. | Graph store of demonstrated paths to assets | Apache AGE on PostgreSQL, Neo4j Community, JanusGraph | control | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: edges only from accepted, reproduced paths; recomputed posture is signed before use |  | [module: Consequence-informed gates beyond tiers](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-consequence-gates) |
| `s5.failure-class-registry` | <a id="r2-part-s5-failure-class-registry"></a>Failure-class registry **(enforces a rule)** | Groups accepted findings into failure classes: the same mistake across services (first-party patterns) or the same weakness in a shared third-party component. A class exists only when at least one finding an independent verifier reproduced sits under it; producers may propose a grouping, but only disposition review accepts one, and a model's judgment never creates or merges a class. Each class records its member findings, the services and components it spans, the consequence its members reach (by reference to the path-impact graph), the road entries that claim to retire it and the closure state of each claim (path broken or constraint enforced). Lives in S5 rather than S6 because a class claim steers road investment and test consolidation, so it must be accepted evidence that producers and the road team cannot write. For the secure-creation loop it sends each class's existence, definition and fix rule while any member finding is open, withholding services and components spanned and highest consequence tier until every member's finding is closed. Per-class member counts go only to the policy-owning team: people, never an agent, never to an agent-reachable part or the model route, even after all members close. Its definition and fix rule name no service, component, path or file of the organization's own, and a third-party class names the component without its versions until a fix exists. A class that gains an open member again sends its next version without spans and consequence tier. The model provider is a reader through the coding agent's model route, so the projection's limits apply to it too. A class's projection version increases when its definition, fix rule, fix availability, road claims, derived-check references, weakness identifiers or disclosed spans or consequence tier change, or when it enters or leaves the all-members-closed state; membership changes while already open do not by themselves increase that version. | Relational class registry linked to the ledger (append-only versions) | PostgreSQL, DefectDojo finding groups as existing-tool rung | control | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: classes only from reproduced findings; versions append, never overwrite |  | [module: Paved road](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-paved-road) |
| `s5.road-assurance-record` | <a id="r2-part-s5-road-assurance-record"></a>Road assurance record **(enforces a rule)** | One record per road entry version: the deep vetting that entry version received before anything counts as covered-by-road. Holds the independent verifier's verdict for each class the entry claims to retire (varied-path fixtures, constraint-enforced or not), the road-level regression results, the scope the vetting covered (APIs, configurations, call patterns), the security-relevant defaults of the frameworks and components the entry depends on as checked at that version (an insecure dependency default voids the claim it touches), and known limits. Vetting runs once per version because an entry is more stable than the business logic built on it; a new version, or a new version of a dependency whose defaults it relies on, starts unvetted and is re-vetted, and a leak found in use (F28) withdraws the record. Written only by the independent verifier and disposition review, so neither the road team nor producers can mark their own entry vetted. For each entry version the status it sends the secure-creation loop is current or not current, with no class outcomes and no reason for a status change, including a failed vetting or a withdrawal after a leak. It projects vetted scope and known limits as API and configuration identifiers and named pattern classes, never an input shape or sequence, test input, fixture content, expected outcome or bypass description. These limits also apply to what reaches the model provider through the coding agent's model route. | Versioned, signed assurance record per road entry version | in-toto attestations with Sigstore, PostgreSQL append-only table | control | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: written only by the independent verifier and disposition review; no current record means no call site is covered-by-road |  | [module: Paved road](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-paved-road) |
| `s6.fix-cap-refusal-log` | <a id="r2-part-s6-fix-cap-refusal-log"></a>Fix-cap refusal log **(enforces a rule)** | One record for each validated finding the fix-cap gate refused entry to an owning team's queue because the team's open validated findings were already at its fix cap, and one for each finding that entered over the cap (its path reaches an asset at or above the consequence line, a sourced exploitation signal matched it, it carries an external disclosure deadline, entered while the gate was down on one of those grounds, or the security risk owner sent it): team, finding id and path key, reason, date, the cap in force with the security role that agreed it (or Ratchet's default cap and the role that set it), the team's open validated findings at the time, and the reviews of a refusal: when, who reviewed it, and the outcome (queued once the team is below its cap, held to the next review, cap revised by the team with security, or the weakness class sent to the paved road). Only the gate writes refusals and over-cap records. The owning team reviews its refusals with the security role that agreed its cap at the profile's interval, monthly unless the organization sets another; the review may run asynchronously in the tracker, and each review record is co-signed by that security role, without which it does not count. A refused finding held at two consecutive reviews goes to the security risk owner, who sends it into the queue over the cap or records a risk acceptance with an expiry; when the acceptance expires, the finding returns to the next review. A refusal past the interval without a review is reported to Ratchet decision owner, who holds both sides to agreed caps. A refused finding stays accepted in the finding ledger and counts in every measure; a refusal never dismisses or drops it. Append-only. | Append-only refusal records with a review field, often a tracker label and saved view at first | PostgreSQL append-only table, DefectDojo or the issue tracker as existing-tool rung | control | Orchestration | queue and retry: a refusal is written before the finding is held back; while the log is down the finding waits with the security side, never routed past the cap and never dropped; reviews count only with the co-signature of the security role that agreed the cap; append-only | existing tool first | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s5.fix-brief-builder` | <a id="r2-part-s5-fix-brief-builder"></a>Fix brief builder **(enforces a rule)** | Writes the description of a weakness that a fixing agent or a fix drafter receives. A brief is either written by a person, and recorded with its author and time, or derived by rule, with no model call, only from the weakness a signed proof condition names (its class, its location and the boundary that failed), a reference to the model-free reproduction's stored receipt, and the receipt fields the independent verifier constrains (step template ids, request and response status classes, assertion results, hashes). Before it issues a derived brief, it checks by rule that the brief's outsider label set covers the labels on its inputs; a derived brief carries none, so a weakness whose description needs free-text steps, the target's output or any other labeled field goes to a person, who writes the brief. It never carries outsider text, the output of a model that read outsider text, the proof condition's expected outcomes or what would show the weakness is not real, or the kept regression. Each brief records how it was made: the author, or the builder's version and the ids of the proof condition and reproduction it came from, and when. A brief the builder cannot derive waits for a person to write it. The share of briefs a person wrote, and the derivations sent to a person because a labeled field was needed, are reported each quarter. | Rule-based brief generator over the proof-condition and reproduction records, with a form for person-written briefs | workflow on Temporal with templates, the tracker's issue form under the evidence identity | control | Evidence integrity | degrade to manual: a person writes the brief; no model-written description replaces it, and a finding waits for its brief rather than going out without one |  | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s5.learning-store` | <a id="r2-part-s5-learning-store"></a>Learning store **(enforces a rule)** | Holds what the loop learns from its own validations and retrieves into producer context through the context compiler: worked reproductions, verdicts, and the hypotheses that held or failed, per judgment type. Only disposition review writes it; no producer or model has a write path. Partitioned by jurisdiction and business unit like the evidence it comes from, each partition under its own key, and a retrieval returns only items from the partition of the snapshot that asks; a read across partitions goes only through the review console. Each item carries its provenance: who confirmed it (a person, or the earned-autonomy grant it was validated under, labeled self-validated), the inputs it came from, and every outsider label on those inputs with its clearing record (the person, or the model-free check and its version, the time, and the fields cleared), and disposition review signs it. Provenance and signature are checked again at retrieval, by rule and with no model call. The store recomputes each item's outsider label set from its input ids when the item is written and again at retrieval, and compares it with the set the item records. An item with no provenance, an item whose outsider label was never cleared, an item whose recorded labels do not cover its inputs' labels, an item validated under a grant that has lapsed, and an item whose grant status the store cannot read are withheld from producer context and logged as flagged retrievals. Flagged retrievals are reported each week. The share that trace to untagged stored items is reported each quarter as the trend and should fall, and the label mismatches found at write and at retrieval are reported each quarter; a rising share or any mismatch means items are entering without their full provenance. Append-only. | Relational append-only store of learned items with provenance, read through a rule-based retrieval check | PostgreSQL append-only tables, Open Policy Agent for the retrieval rule | control | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: append-only; written only by disposition review; while it is unavailable producers run without learned items, and nothing is retrieved around the provenance check; an item validated under a grant whose status the store cannot read is withheld as if the grant had lapsed |  | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s5.exposed-secret-intake` | <a id="r2-part-s5-exposed-secret-intake"></a>Exposed-secret intake **(enforces a rule)** | Receives every exposed-credential hit: a secret scanner's export, or a credential a person found and entered at its own form, so exposed secrets need no tool-portfolio module. Before anything stores the hit it replaces the value with a fingerprint and keeps the issuer, the identifier, where it was exposed, and the scan rule and rule version; no lead, record, model request or other part ever receives the value from it. It looks up the credential type's verification recipe in the regression catalog, and only when that recipe needs a status probe does it seal the value under the owning team's key in key management, to be released to the credential lease broker for that probe series and destroyed after it. Deterministic, no model. A hit it cannot parse is held with its value sealed and alerts the evidence owner; it is never forwarded with the value. | Deterministic redaction and fingerprinting service with a sealing client | a secret scanner's own redaction, OpenBao transit engine | control | Control safety | fail closed: a hit it cannot fingerprint is held sealed and alerts the evidence owner; the value is never forwarded |  | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |
| `s6.kept-test-mirror` | <a id="r2-part-s6-kept-test-mirror"></a>Kept-test mirror **(enforces a rule)** | Security automatically inventories every kept test, both the team-owned instance tests and metadata for the independent aggregate and class-level checks. Source credentials can only read repositories and CI history. The mirror can append observations and submit drift findings but cannot write a team repository, pull request, suite, test status or policy. Enrollment is append-only, so a removed test remains in the expected inventory. Each observation binds the test id and version, finding and class, team and suite, repository revision, dependency scope, configuration, run and result, observed time, source and collection outcome. Removed, skipped and failing tests each submit a distinct drift finding to the normal loop, deduplicated by test version and drift episode. Unreachable sources, missing runs, stale observations and missing dependencies read unknown. A retired test retains its history and the authorized lifecycle decision. Near-JIT risk review reads current protection only when a passing enabled test was actually run at the revision, configuration and dependency scope being assessed within the review's stated freshness window. Passing instance evidence never substitutes for independent aggregate validation or grants authority. The mirror emits metadata and source references only, never protected test content. It reports contributed-test survival and promotion to class-level guardrails with observation coverage and cohort denominators. | Append-only read-only inventory of test sources and run observations | Security-owned metadata index with read-only source credentials | control | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: Missing or stale observations mean unknown protection. Source writes are denied. | existing tool first | [core: evidence plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evidence) |

An earned-autonomy grant or a promotion never reaches past the design limit for its judgment type or producer: the record can widen what a grant covers only up to that limit, and a request past it is refused. | `s7.flow-cost-telemetry` | <a id="r2-part-s7-flow-cost-telemetry"></a>Flow/cost/operational telemetry | Queues, WIP, age, cycle time and unsuccessful work; provider/serving, fixture, context and remediation costs. Headline flow metric: handoffs per closed loop and queue time per handoff, against the profile's ceiling per claim. Attestation load as owner minutes and testing-team drafting hours per attestation. Delivery guardrail for each service under test uses DORA's current five metrics: change lead time, deployment frequency, failed deployment recovery time, change fail rate and deployment rework rate. Fix-capacity saturation signal: per owning team, accepted findings still open past the profile's remediation window, the trend of median accepted-finding age, and open accepted findings against the team's WIP cap; the signal is on when the age trend rises for the profile's window while open findings sit at the cap. Verification hours: intake, reproduction, disposition review and retest hours per accepted closure and per rejected candidate, counted for every arm. Remediation measures beside DORA's five: escape rate (security weaknesses reaching production per deployment, by weakness class, from the finding ledger and deploy records), time to remediate (reproduced finding to fix verified in the running system, from the finding ledger, the tracker and deploy records), remediation change fail rate (deployments of security fixes that cause a failure), cost per validated novel finding, validated findings arriving against verified fixes per weakness class, queue age of validated findings, the share of validated findings that are novel against recurring (matched on the finding ledger's path key), and weakness classes retired. Kept-test suite health: tests per lane and status, quarantine count and age against the organization's caps, re-proof results, and retirements by covering check. The escape rate counts only confirmed escape records detected within the organization's detection window, and is shown a second time with late escapes included. Escapes with no attributable deployment are counted in a bucket of their own, apart from both rates. Unconfirmed escapes are listed apart with their age, and each one older than the profile's alert age (the detection window when none is set) alerts the security risk owner. Beside each count of escapes sit their consequences: the consequence tiers of the assets reached and the services affected, time to recover for each, and how many fixes removed the weakness class against one instance. Agent actions against their limits: for every agent action, the two predictors written before it ran (its route's trailing accuracy over the organization's window, and the tier its limit allows with the action's class), its authority, what it touched, and its accuracy (from the independent check, or from the sampled check for an action that ran on a grant alone), and for each wrong action its cost measured apart from both predictors (time to recover and the incident severity the defensive side sets); the phase 2 report states which predictor better forecasts that cost, or that the sample is too small to decide. Fix-path cost: per owning team, the wait age of each validated finding still waiting with the security side for a complete fix path, and the security side's hours to prepare each fix path. Held dependencies: per control, the held findings it holds, their tests' last runs against their intervals, and the share of held-dependency tests whose run converted their finding, which measures how often a credited control fails. In-tier wait: per consequence tier, the time from a lead or scenario entering the tier's queue to its test starting, per path key, with the path keys a typed decision ordered last in consecutive windows listed beside it; each lead or scenario past the dial register's in-tier wait limit goes to the sampling service to be drawn. These are measures and signals only; the organization sets every threshold, and no measure widens a design limit. From the kept-test mirror, report contributed-test survival and promotion from instance to class with numerator, denominator, cohort, window and observation coverage. Missing observations read unknown and never imply zero drift or current protection. | Flow and cost metrics store | Apache DevLake, OpenTelemetry into ClickHouse | evaluation | Best effort and analytics | continue without: gaps flagged; gate scripts fail on missing rows | existing tool first | [core: evaluation plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evaluation) |

Reports time-to-adopt per entry and the top escape-hatch reasons beside the hatch rate, so a rising rate can read as a missing road capability as well as drift; hatch reasons go to the road owner's backlog as product demand. Owned by evaluation, so the road team never grades its own road. Yield is judged per rule or vulnerability class on the organization's own code; a tool-level figure is only an aggregate of those. Yield: among a sample of leads drawn by the sampling service from a pre-registered random frame per tool class and rule family (frozen before results exist), the share the loop demonstrates when tested regardless of whether an agent chose them; untested leads are never counted as false. Miss rate: demonstrated paths in the tool's scope that no lead from the tool pointed at. Review claims: threat-model and review invariants contradicted by a demonstrated path. Reports intervals and sample sizes, keeps unresolved cases in the denominator, and draws no conclusion below the pre-registered minimum. Owned by evaluation, so no tool team grades its own tool. | Sampling and estimation job over the lead store and path graph | Dagster job with DuckDB, Jupyter for review | evaluation | Best effort and analytics | continue without: no yield conclusion below the pre-registered sample minimum; untested leads never counted false |  | [module: Existing security tools as leads](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-tool-portfolio) |
| `s7.tool-delivery-impact` | <a id="r2-part-s7-tool-delivery-impact"></a>Tool delivery-impact evaluator | Measures what each tool costs the delivery system, as a side-quest metric beside yield. Direct measures from pipeline timings and tracker data: stage time and queue time attributable to each tool's gate, deploys blocked or delayed by the tool, and developer triage time per lead. Associations with DORA's five metrics (change lead time, deployment frequency, failed deployment recovery time, change fail rate, deployment rework rate) for services by tool configuration, reported as correlational only, never causal, with the comparison set and confounders named. Uses only the delivery metrics the profile says the organization already collects; a missing metric is reported as not measured, never estimated. Evidence for portfolio decisions, never a decision. Owned by evaluation, so no tool team grades its own tool. | Delivery metrics job over pipeline and tracker data | Apache DevLake DORA metrics, Four Keys, OpenTelemetry CI/CD spans | evaluation | Best effort and analytics | continue without: a metric the organization does not collect is reported as not measured, never estimated; correlations labeled as such | existing tool first | [module: Existing security tools as leads](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-tool-portfolio) |
| `s7.tool-portfolio-decisions` | <a id="r2-part-s7-tool-portfolio-decisions"></a>Tool portfolio decision record **(enforces a rule)** | One record per tool (and per rule family where useful): integrate as a signal source, migrate the function into the loop or onto the paved road, or deprecate. Each decision cites the yield and miss-rate report, the developer toil (queue items and triage hours), the delivery-impact report (gate time, blocked deploys, labeled correlations with DORA's five metrics: change lead time, deployment frequency, failed deployment recovery time, change fail rate, deployment rework rate) and the cost, names the accountable decider (security risk owner by default, profile may change) and has a review date. Tools the profile flags as obligation-mandated cannot be deprecated outright: their deprecation means keep running for the obligation and remove from developer queues. Evaluation supplies evidence; it never decides. | Versioned decision register with attestation | Git with branch protection, PostgreSQL | control | Best effort and analytics | continue without: decisions are versioned records; an outage delays decisions, never changes a tool's state | existing tool first | [module: Existing security tools as leads](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-tool-portfolio) |
| `s7.sampling-service` | <a id="r2-part-s7-sampling-service"></a>Sampling and seeding service | The one place Ratchet samples and seeds. Registers a frame, freezes it in the snapshot and cohort manager before results exist, draws reproducibly from a recorded seed, dispatches each draw as a validation request through the normal S3 envelope, and reports with intervals and sample sizes. Frames: model-made rejections, dedup merges and not-applicable calls, covered-by-road call sites, lead yield and held leads. Seed sets: planted known-true findings, received as fixture references and hashes only (assertions stay in S5; the independent verifier compares), each planted behind outsider text in a held-out fixture, so that a crafted report, tool lead, ticket or code comment sits in the path model triage reads, as it would for a real lead; seeded known-bad proposals sent to the action broker at the dial register's rate, indistinguishable from real proposals at the approval console; and seeded clearing requests carrying a planted instruction, sent at the same rate to the people who clear outsider text and indistinguishable from real ones at the review console. The suppression rate is the share of planted known-true findings that model triage rejected, merged as a duplicate, held below the bar or called not applicable, reported with its interval and sample size to the independent security staff; a planted finding with no outsider text in its path is not counted in it. Seeded proposals measure gate health; detecting what nobody flagged stays with the sampling frames and held-out exercises, never with approvers. Every human gate the four rules name is seeded: beside the approver's seeded proposals, the proof-condition holder receives seeded results that do not meet their proof condition, and the decider receives seeded findings and closures that should not be accepted, each indistinguishable from real ones and sent at the rate the organization's profile sets for that gate. Seeds are drawn from the stated categories of the organization's threat model for its own oversight and refreshed by the date the profile records, so people at a gate cannot learn to spot a fixed set. Outcomes come back from the approval service, the review console and the finding ledger. Per-person catch rates go to the seeding owner, who sits outside the gate's reporting line, and to each person for their own rate, never to a manager or a performance review; sponsors and managers see pooled rates. The service computes each breach of a gate's catch-rate floor from the recorded outcomes and opens the breach action the profile names; no person decides whether a breach happened. Rates come from the dial register and the profile and never fall below their floors without a recorded acceptance. The negative-challenge frame is drawn by consequence tier and by age, so a lead a model buried still reaches a person or a deterministic re-check: every tier draws at least the negative-challenge share, each tier at or above the consequence line at least the re-check floor the dial register sets, and every lead a model marked duplicate, low or not applicable, and every scenario a typed decision ordered last, that has waited in its tier past the register's in-tier wait limit is drawn whatever the sample. Flip-style seeds test the typed decision routes the way seeded proposals test approvers: pairs of planted leads, known-true and known-false, identical but for added text chosen to move a classifier's verdict (short appended token sequences, padding), placed like the planted findings and sent into each typed decision route at the seeded-proposal rate. A route's flip rate is the share of its pairs whose two typed decisions differ, reported per route with its interval and sample size as gate health to the independent security staff and the reliability monitor; a known-true lead that a route marked duplicate, low or not applicable counts in the suppression rate too. Owned by evaluation, so no frame's subject grades itself. | Sampling and seeding job with pre-registered frames (seeded draws, frozen manifests) | Dagster, Apache Airflow | evaluation | Best effort and analytics | continue without: a missed draw is reported against its floor; a floor-bearing draw that misses by a day alerts and the gap counts against the floor; a frame is never redrawn after results exist |  | [core: evaluation plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evaluation) |
| `s7.value-ledger` | <a id="r2-part-s7-value-ledger"></a>Business-unit value ledger | For each business unit: cost paid (attestation minutes and drafting hours, regression stage time in its pipelines, triage, verification hours per accepted closure and per rejected candidate, closure acceptances, product-side hours per accepted finding), risk reduced on its assets (demonstrated paths to its crown-jewel and critical assets broken or constraint-enforced, regressions current, closures labeled by environment), friction removed (scanner findings and triage removed by the road and the developer-queue filter, analyst review time saved, test load avoided), and things that went right (first-run regression pass share, legitimate-function check pass share, false-failure rate, time to adapt a regression after a legitimate change). Sampled escapes are subtracted from every friction and risk figure. The quarterly status report reads from it, and the business risk owner sees it for their business unit. | Analytical store and report per business unit | PostgreSQL, DuckDB, Apache Superset | evaluation | Best effort and analytics | continue without: gaps flagged per business unit; sampled escapes subtracted before any friction or risk figure |  | [core: evaluation plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evaluation) |
| `s7.failover-drill` | <a id="r2-part-s7-failover-drill"></a>Failover drill | Keeps every fallback route qualified before it is needed. On the dial-register interval (default 30 days) and after any change to a fallback route's model, serving stack or data, dispatches the pre-registered seeded cases as ordinary validation requests pinned to the self-hosted route (through the normal S3 envelope and the request-data gate), compares outcomes, cost and verification hours with the primary route's last qualified run, and sends the comparison to promotion review, which requalifies, narrows or demotes the fallback. A fallback whose last drill is older than the interval is marked stale in the route registry, and the route broker treats a stale fallback as unavailable, so a run pauses or drops to the next rung instead of using it. Owned by evaluation, so the serving team never grades its own route. | Scheduled drill job that replays seeded cases on the fallback route and files the comparison for promotion review | Apache Airflow, Dagster | evaluation | Best effort and analytics | continue without: a missed drill is reported and alerts once a day overdue; a fallback past its drill interval is marked stale and the route broker treats it as unavailable |  | [core: evaluation plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evaluation) |
| `s7.reliability-monitor` | <a id="r2-part-s7-reliability-monitor"></a>Reliability monitor | Ratchet's own service-level objectives and error budgets (the design model ratchet_slos): approval latency, grant success, intake-to-verified time, evidence durability, posture freshness and canary freshness. Pages the on-call operator roles; a silent loop canary trips an external dead-man alarm (F65); a floor-bearing sampling draw or failover drill that misses by a day alerts and the gap counts against its floor (F67). When a budget burns it freezes Ratchet's own releases through the policy release pipeline; it never blocks a product deploy. Missing indicators read as unknown and page, never as healthy. Opens a postmortem record whenever a named trigger fires (the postmortem triggers in Ratchet's operating policy), owned by the on-call role of the first failing part. The review is blameless: it states contributing causes as conditions of the system, never as a person, and turns each action item into a design change, a profile change or a work unit with an owner. The security risk owner reads and signs each one, and an open postmortem stays on the status report until it closes. | Service-level objective and error-budget monitor with an external dead-man alarm | Prometheus with Sloth, Alertmanager, Healthchecks.io self-hosted | evaluation | Orchestration | mark unknown: missing indicators page as unknown, never healthy; a silent loop canary trips the external dead-man alarm | existing tool first | [core: evaluation plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-evaluation) |

### Paved road plane (2 parts)

The shared platform paved road engineers build on (portal templates, base images, libraries, gateway policies, secure defaults).

| Code | Part | What it does | Technology class | Open-source examples | Trust zone | Tier | When a dependency fails | Flags | Core or module |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `s6.paved-road-catalog` | <a id="r2-part-s6-paved-road-catalog"></a>Paved-road catalog | The shared platform's catalog of abstractions engineers build on: developer-portal software templates and scorecards, base images and container platform policies, API and authentication libraries, gateway policies, secure defaults and linters. Each entry is versioned and declares the failure classes it claims to retire, its enforcement kind (enforced: compile-time check, framework default or platform policy that applies without the engineer choosing it; opt-in: a library or template the engineer must choose to use), how a service proves it uses the entry, and its escape hatches. Enforced entries are preferred when choosing what to put on the road. An opt-in entry confers covered-by-road status only at call sites the deterministic per-call-site conformance check proves. Every escape-hatch use records a reason, and the reasons feed the road owner's backlog as product demand. Entries change only through the platform's normal review and release path; a candidate road change from a producer is a proposal, never a merge. A retirement claim counts only after the independent verifier confirms it with varied-path fixtures. | Developer portal software catalog with templates and scorecards | Backstage software templates and Tech Insights, Chainguard-style minimal base images, Envoy Gateway policies | shared:existing | Orchestration | queue and retry: entries change only through the platform release path; a catalog outage blocks road changes, never service deploys | swap point, existing tool first | [module: Paved road](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-paved-road) |
| `s6.road-conformance-check` | <a id="r2-part-s6-road-conformance-check"></a>Road conformance check **(enforces a rule)** | Proves road use per call site or code path, never per service or dependency manifest. A deterministic static rule or build-time check classifies each in-scope call site as using a vetted road entry version or not (for example, every HTML output goes through the vetted encoding library, every outbound call through the governed client), and deployed-state provenance confirms the built artifact matches what was checked (image digest lineage, resolved library versions, observed gateway routes). A call site the check cannot classify is off-road. Only entry versions with a current road assurance record count. Covered-by-road applies to the classes the entry retires, for the conforming call sites only: those call sites inherit the entry's road-level regression and are labeled covered for analysis tailoring; every other call site in the same service keeps per-service regressions and full analysis. Escape-hatch use or a failed check reinstates the per-service regressions for those classes and records the event. | Policy-as-code conformance over deployed state and SBOMs | Kyverno, Conftest with Open Policy Agent, Syft SBOMs | control | Evidence integrity | mark unknown: unknown conformance means off-road: per-service regressions stay in force | swap point | [module: Paved road](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-paved-road) |

### Integration plane (43 parts)

Adapters to the organization's tracker, CI/CD, IAM, change management, SIEM, and external systems.

| Code | Part | What it does | Technology class | Open-source examples | Trust zone | Tier | When a dependency fails | Flags | Core or module |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `s1.source-adapters` | <a id="r2-part-s1-source-adapters"></a>Source adapters | SCM/build/deploy/IAM APIs; bounded paging, cursors and source-specific time windows. For change provenance, verifies each attestation's signature against keys the organization registers and records the verification status with the record; a stamp that cannot be verified is kept as unverified, never dropped. It fills a change's declared facts (person, tool, model, session and skill-bundle hash) only from the stamp it verified, so measurement never reads a fact the signature did not cover. | Connector and change-data-capture adapters | Debezium, Apache Camel, Airbyte | reasoning | Orchestration | mark unknown: collector outage marks facts unknown, never current | swap point | [core: integration plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-shared:integration) |
| `s6.hardening-service` | <a id="r2-part-s6-hardening-service"></a>Hardening and regression service | Receive an accepted scoped finding; produce reviewed ChangeRecord, actual-deployment correlation, independent retest disposition and RegressionSpec. For each validated finding, proposes a detection of the demonstrated path's observable behavior to the defensive side, which reviews and owns it. Once the fix-cap gate admits a finding, prepares its fix path with security and the owning engineer before it enters the owning team's queue: the reproducing test from the replay of the demonstrated path (or a reproduction through the running system where no build-level test can reproduce it), the drafted fix or the reason none is possible, and the regression test or repeated reproduction that becomes the owned gate, with its named owner. Records the security side's hours for each fix path. The drafted fix starts from the finding's fix brief, and from the disposition it takes only identifiers, statuses, enumerated fields and references. Security, the owning engineer and an agent co-create the correction and its component-local instance regression as one pull request. The instance test lives in the owning team's suite and the owning team owns it. A newly discovered variant enters as a new finding through intake, independent reproduction and disposition. Nobody quietly changes the team's pull request. The independent aggregate validation stays with security. Its proof conditions, chained paths, protected fixtures and expected outcomes never reach the fixing agent. A local instance test contains no such material. There is no separate variant probe set. | Fix-path drafting through the organization's PR and review workflow | Forgejo, Gitea | reasoning | Orchestration | queue and retry: organization's release workflow | existing tool first | [core: integration plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-shared:integration) |
| `s6.owner-change-connector` | <a id="r2-part-s6-owner-change-connector"></a>Owner/change connector | Route root cause to code/IAM/network/defense owner; draft candidate PR or policy change, with proof and safe rollback. The connector is the core's fix drafter, whether a person or an agent drafts the change through it: its only description of the weakness is the finding's fix brief (FixBrief), and from the disposition it takes only identifiers, statuses, enumerated fields and references, so no free text a producer or a model wrote reaches the draft. Security and the owning team co-create the fix path: a validated finding enters an owning team's queue only with a complete fix path (FixPath) co-created by security, the owning engineer and an agent, made of a reproducing test that fails on the current build, or a reproduction through the running system where no build-level test can reproduce the weakness; a drafted fix the owning team takes, edits or declines with a reason, or "no draft possible" with the reason recorded, which goes to the owning team's lead and the security risk owner; and an owned gate, the regression test or repeated reproduction the fix must pass, with its named owner. Before the fix path is prepared, the connector sends the validated finding to the fix-cap gate, and prepares the path only for a finding the gate admits; the complete path goes back through the gate for queue entry. An incomplete path waits with the security side; the wait is measured per owning team, and a finding still waiting past the profile's remediation window is reported to Ratchet decision owner. The connector applies no cap and writes no refusal. While the fix-cap gate is unavailable, it prepares the fix path for, and sends straight to the owning team, only a validated finding whose disposition records a pass ground, a field disposition review writes and the connector cannot; it holds every other finding with the security side. When the gate returns, it hands the gate every finding it sent during the outage, with the pass ground it sent it on. Security, the owning engineer and an agent co-create the correction and its component-local instance regression as one pull request. The instance test lives in the owning team's suite and the owning team owns it. A newly discovered variant enters as a new finding through intake, independent reproduction and disposition. Nobody quietly changes the team's pull request. The independent aggregate validation stays with security. Its proof conditions, chained paths, protected fixtures and expected outcomes never reach the fixing agent. A local instance test contains no such material. There is no separate variant probe set. | Tracker and SCM connector | Apache Camel, custom webhook service | reasoning | Orchestration | queue and retry: exactly one linked change per accepted finding (idempotency key = finding id) | swap point, existing tool first | [core: integration plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-shared:integration) |
| `s6.enterprise-adapters` | <a id="r2-part-s6-enterprise-adapters"></a>Existing CI/release/SOC adapters | Existing tracker, CI/CD, IAM, change-management and SIEM integrations. Carries each accepted regression into the owning service's pipeline as a stage (advisory, then rejecting) together with its signed failure posture, the tier defaults for expired postures and its declared dependency scope from the gate failure policy, and reports gate outcomes per path back to it. At deploy time the stage presents the window bundle minted before the window to the execution cell and receives the replay verdict back, so no control-plane service has to answer during the deploy. Adapters are contract-only glue: each translates between an organization tool and one model contract and holds no program logic; each adapter is its own unit with a contract test, and the adapter count per organization is reported. | Adapter set for tracker, CI/CD, IAM, change management and SIEM | Apache Camel, CloudEvents adapters | shared:existing | Orchestration | posture from policy: regression stage applies the signed posture when the platform cannot answer | swap point, existing tool first | [core: integration plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-shared:integration) |
| `ext.estate` | <a id="r2-part-ext-estate"></a>Existing estate and facts | Assets, IAM, repositories, artifacts, deployments; authoritative ownership and source versions. | The organization's systems under validation |  | shared:existing |  |  | existing tool first | [core: integration plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-shared:integration) |
| `ext.soc-incident-response` | <a id="r2-part-ext-soc-incident-response"></a>SOC / incident response | Corroborate offensive receipts; visibility gaps; incident determination. For an attack injection into a deployed test environment or production, records when its monitoring or response detected the injected weakness; under the trusted-agent notice mode, one named role on this side is told in advance and can stop the exercise at any point; the stop goes straight to the action control service, which revokes the injection grant, so an injected weakness is never handled as a real incident past that point. When the action control service cannot answer, the trusted agent stops the exercise through a second path that does not cross the control plane: the service owner's own rollback, named in the runbook, which withdraws the injected change; the cell's timer still runs the restore at the time limit. | The organization's SOC, SIEM and incident response | Wazuh, OpenSearch Security Analytics | shared:existing |  |  | existing tool first | [core: integration plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-shared:integration) |
| `s7.assurance-incident-integration` | <a id="r2-part-s7-assurance-incident-integration"></a>Assurance and incident integration **(enforces a rule)** | Supports independent staff review, formal testing schedules, residual-risk decisions, board reporting and incident determination; an agent verdict may assist but does not replace the responsible institutional authority. | SIEM and case-management integration | TheHive, Shuffle | control | Orchestration | queue and retry: incident signals never suppressed; queue on outage and alert | swap point, existing tool first | [core: integration plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-shared:integration) |
| `ext.threat-intelligence` | <a id="r2-part-ext-threat-intelligence"></a>Threat intelligence and exploitation signals | Attacker-side signals (observed exploitation, known-exploited vulnerability lists, threat reporting) brought in as sourced facts. Where the organization wires a vulnerability database, it also states the affected version ranges for a listed vulnerability identifier, with the record they came from. | Threat intelligence platform and exploitation feeds | MISP, OpenCTI | shared:existing |  |  | swap point, existing tool first | [module: Consequence-informed gates beyond tiers](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-consequence-gates) |
| `ext.appsec-tooling` | <a id="r2-part-ext-appsec-tooling"></a>Existing AppSec tooling and external findings | The organization's existing security tools and external reports: static and dynamic analysis, software composition analysis, secrets, infrastructure-as-code and container scanning, linters, bug bounty submissions and external pentest reports. Each exports findings with its own identifiers and confidence; none of them is evidence in this design until the loop reproduces a path. | The organization's existing AppSec scanners and external finding sources | Semgrep, CodeQL, OWASP ZAP | shared:existing |  |  | swap point, existing tool first | [module: Existing security tools as leads](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-tool-portfolio) |
| `ext.security-review-practice` | <a id="r2-part-ext-security-review-practice"></a>Threat modeling and security review practice | Human security work products: threat models, design and security review outcomes, accepted risk notes. Each carries claims about what a system should prevent (trust boundaries, must-prevent paths) that the loop can test. | The organization's threat-modeling and security review records | OWASP Threat Dragon, pytm | shared:existing |  |  | existing tool first | [module: Existing security tools as leads](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-tool-portfolio) |
| `s1.tool-signal-intake` | <a id="r2-part-s1-tool-signal-intake"></a>Tool signal intake | Imports tool exports and external reports as leads: normalizes each to the ToolLead contract with tool, tool version, rule id, rule version, target, location, tool-reported confidence and observed timestamp, and keeps a mapping when a tool update renames rule ids so provenance survives upgrades. Deduplicates within a tool by fingerprint, never across tools (two tools agreeing is a signal to record, not a merge). Applies per-tool rate limits so a noisy tool cannot crowd out others, and marks a tool's leads stale when its export stops. Never writes source facts, the ledger or dispositions. Secret scanner hits never pass through it: they go to the exposed-secret intake, which keeps the value out of every lead. | Findings import and normalization pipeline (SARIF and vendor exports to a lead schema) | DefectDojo importers, SARIF tooling, Apache Camel | reasoning | Orchestration | mark unknown: a stopped export marks that tool's leads stale; per-tool rate limits keep one noisy tool from crowding out others | swap point, existing tool first | [module: Existing security tools as leads](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-tool-portfolio) |
| `s6.developer-queue-filter` | <a id="r2-part-s6-developer-queue-filter"></a>Developer-queue filter **(enforces a rule)** | Decides which items reach a service team's work queue: every demonstrated finding (through the owner change connector as before), plus leads whose tool and rule clear the profile's confidence bar for that tool class (set from measured yield once it exists, from the profile default before). Leads below the bar stay visible to security and remain in the sampling frame. Leads at call sites the road conformance check marks covered-by-road for the lead's class (a vetted road entry version) are downgraded or held, at call-site granularity only: a lead at an unclassified or off-road call site in the same service routes normally. Holding a lead back is recorded as held, never as dismissed, and an obligation-mandated tool's leads follow the obligation's routing. When the filter is unavailable, no lead reaches developer queues; demonstrated findings still flow. | Rule-based routing policy over the findings pipeline | Open Policy Agent, DefectDojo SLA and push rules | control | Orchestration | fail closed: unavailable means no lead reaches developer queues; demonstrated findings still flow; held leads are never dismissed | swap point | [module: Existing security tools as leads](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-tool-portfolio) |
| `ext.known-exploited-catalog` | <a id="r2-part-ext-known-exploited-catalog"></a>Public known-exploited vulnerability catalog | A public catalog of vulnerabilities with evidence of exploitation in the wild, published as structured data any organization can fetch: per entry, the vulnerability identifier, the vendor or project, the product, the vulnerability's name and the date added. It states no affected versions. Read by the fix-cap gate as structured fields only and matched to findings by rule; it never creates a finding, never lowers a posture and never counts as evidence. Part of the core, so the exploitation pass works without the consequence-gates module. | A public known exploited vulnerabilities catalog, fetched as structured data | CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities Catalog | shared:existing |  |  | swap point, existing tool first | [core: integration plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-shared:integration) |
| `s6.team-regression-suite` | <a id="r2-part-s6-team-regression-suite"></a>Team regression suite | The existing team repository and CI suite own and run each component-local instance regression contributed in the co-created correction pull request. Its test source and local outcomes are visible to coding agents. Only the owning team changes or retires it through normal review. The automatic inventory publishes repository revision, test version, declared dependencies, configuration and actual CI run observations through a read-only source interface. It never receives proof conditions, chained-path validations or protected RegressionSpec content. | Existing owning-team repository and CI suite | The owning team repository and CI runner | shared:existing | Evidence integrity | queue and retry: Missing or stale observations mean unknown protection. Source writes are denied. | existing tool first | [core: integration plane](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-core-shared:integration) |
| `jig:secure-creation` | <a id="r2-part-jig-secure-creation"></a>Jig (secure creation at the developer's edit) | The organization's secure-creation loop, where developers and coding agents write code, drawn as its planes: the context plane that steers what the agent copies, the inner loop of the agent's turn, the commit and push boundary, the pull-request plane and the measurement plane. Ratchet is its learning plane. A sibling design that runs alone or beside this loop. Coding agents run inside it, so it is agent-reachable: it receives proven failure classes, road entries with a current assurance record, derived checks that report pass or fail and fix briefs, and never a kept regression, a proof condition, an expected-outcome hash or text an outsider wrote. Its controlled-environment premise is declared as assumptions, each with the parts that rely on it and a fallback that does not. | The organization's secure-creation loop for developers and coding agents (agent edit hooks, Stop gate, commit, push and pull-request checks) | pre-commit, Semgrep, in-toto attestations | shared:existing |  |  | swap point, existing tool first | [module: Secure creation (Jig)](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-secure-creation) |
| `jig:context-plane` | <a id="r2-part-jig-context-plane"></a>Context plane: paved-road guidance and skills | What the coding agent copies from: paved-road references in the codebase, a skill per paved road, short instruction files under code ownership, the security constraints written into the task's spec for high-risk work, and the diff-size budget per task. Steers toward proven classes and toward road entries whose assurance record is current. Holds proven classes until the policy-owning team turns each into a rule. Guidance only: instruction files are hygiene and enforce nothing. It keeps only the current version of each proven class, replacing any earlier spans and consequence tier when a class gains an open member. It exposes road assurance per entry version only as current or not current, with no class outcomes or failure or withdrawal reasons. The model provider is a reader through the coding agent's model route, and guidance sent on that route obeys the same projection limits. | Paved-road references, skills and instruction files under code ownership, validated in CI | CODEOWNERS, Backstage TechDocs | shared:existing |  |  | existing tool first | [module: Secure creation (Jig)](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-secure-creation) |
| `jig:check-suite` | <a id="r2-part-jig-check-suite"></a>Shared check suite and policy source | The one policy source, compiled into every harness's locked configuration, the git hooks and the server-side required checks, so the same checks at the same versions run at every point from the agent's turn to the pull request. Holds the rules (type checks, linters, diff-scoped analysis, secret scanning, dependency policy, authorization-pattern rules), the derived checks Ratchet sends, and each rule the policy-owning team turned from a proven class with its class test. Logs every derived check it drops or disables. It gates on a rule turned from a proven class only once a class test at the rule's version has passed (the rule caught every member it ran against, and ran against at least one), and until then the rule steers and reports. It receives only rule id, rule version and pass or fail for each class test, and passes only that binary result to the measurement plane; no per-class counts reach it. | One versioned policy source compiled into harness configuration, git hooks and required checks | pre-commit, Semgrep, Gitleaks | shared:existing |  |  | swap point, existing tool first | [module: Secure creation (Jig)](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-secure-creation) |
| `jig:inner-loop` | <a id="r2-part-jig-inner-loop"></a>Inner loop: the agent's turn | Where the coding agent works in a developer's session, inside a harness, with findings returned to the agent while it is still working and a gate before it may end its turn. | The developer's coding session with its agent harness |  | shared:existing |  |  | existing tool first | [module: Secure creation (Jig)](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-secure-creation) |
| `jig:coding-agent` | <a id="r2-part-jig-coding-agent"></a>Coding agent session | Writes code in a developer's session. Reads repository code and mirrored dependencies, the context plane, the findings of the per-edit checks and the stop gate, fix briefs, and typed answers from the quarantined reader. It reads no outsider text itself: issue text, web pages and dependency documentation go to the reader. Its changes are proposals that merge only through the protected branch. Its egress goes only to approved model routes and the organization's mirrors. | Coding agent session in an approved harness |  | shared:existing |  |  | swap point, existing tool first | [module: Secure creation (Jig)](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-secure-creation) |
| `jig:harness-full-parity` | <a id="r2-part-jig-harness-full-parity"></a>Coding harness with full parity (example) | A worked example of a coding harness that runs the agent under a locked managed policy, with blocking hooks, a stop gate, full session telemetry linked to each commit, and a pinned version. | Coding harness with locked managed policy, blocking hooks, stop gate, telemetry export and version pinning |  | shared:existing |  |  | swap point, existing tool first | [module: Secure creation (Jig)](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-secure-creation) |
| `jig:harness-no-pinning` | <a id="r2-part-jig-harness-no-pinning"></a>Coding harness without version pinning (example) | A worked example of a coding harness with locked requirements, hooks and a stop gate, partial session telemetry and no way to pin its own version, so the server-side run of the suite is the version of record for its changes. | Coding harness with locked requirements, hooks and stop gate, partial telemetry and no version pinning |  | shared:existing |  |  | swap point, existing tool first | [module: Secure creation (Jig)](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-secure-creation) |
| `jig:harness-no-stop-gate` | <a id="r2-part-jig-harness-no-stop-gate"></a>Coding harness without a stop gate (example) | A worked example of a coding harness with blocking hooks, no stop gate and no telemetry export. The same suite runs at commit through the locked pre-commit gate in place of the stop gate, and its changes count as unlabeled in every provenance measure. | Coding harness with blocking hooks and no stop gate or telemetry export |  | shared:existing |  |  | swap point, existing tool first | [module: Secure creation (Jig)](R0-building-blocks.md#r0-block-secure-creation) |
| `jig:edit-checks` | <a id="r2-part-jig-edit-checks"></a>Per-edit checks | Type checks, linters, fast secure-pattern rules and the derived checks that fit the per-edit budget, run on each edit with the results returned to the agent's context.
