| PromotionDecision | Route or producer configuration id and version, decision (promote, narrow, demote, requalify), evidence references, deciding authority (never evaluation engineering), decided-at. | versioned record: configuration id plus version; superseded, never overwritten | s7.promotion-review | s4.qualification-route-registry, s2.approved-producers |
| GateOutcome | Per gate and path: outage holds, deploys let through with a record, ship-throughs with acceptance ids, post-recovery replay results, deploys held without a regression failure, posture and window-bundle expiries. Feeds the posture review. | at-least-once: keyed by gate, path and event | s6.enterprise-adapters | s3.gate-failure-policy |
| RouteQualification | Route id, rung (frontier, self-hosted, no-agent, manual), qualification status, last passed drill date, stale flag, and for a self-hosted route the digest of the weights it is pinned to. Structured fields only. | latest-wins record: route id; history kept | s4.qualification-route-registry | s7.failover-drill, s4.request-data-gate, s4.model-data-gateway |
| TypedDecision | One model judgment of a declared decision type: decision_type (lead-triage \| queue-confidence \| scenario-order \| tier-draft \| gate-health \| output-flag), subject reference, choice from the type's fixed answer set, stated probability, route id and version, the qualification record it ran under, and fallback_used. Advisory by construction: it may rank, route or flag, never authorize, count as coverage, set a verdict, close a finding or attest a tier. Inputs that carry attacker-written text keep their untrusted marking; the choice itself is structured. | at-least-once: keyed by decision type, subject and route version; advisory, so a lost or duplicate decision changes a ranking or a flag, never a grant, verdict or closure | s2.typed-decision-worker | s1.tool-lead-store, s6.developer-queue-filter, s2.workflow-worker-app, s2.operator-interface, s7.reliability-monitor, s7.matched-evaluation-executor, s7.sampling-service |
| SourceRecord | One normalized record from a source system: source system and record id, record kind (change, build, deploy, identity, component inventory, threat signal), observed or declared, observed-at, the query window and cursor it came from, returned and total-or-unknown, truncation, and a reference to the raw record. Untrusted text in a source record keeps its untrusted marking. The form in which the source adapters hand facts to the rest of the context plane. | at-least-once: keyed by source system, record id and cursor window; a re-read appends, never overwrites | s1.source-adapters, s1.source-facts | s1.identity-validity-resolver, s1.source-facts, s5.path-impact-graph, s5.failure-class-registry, s1.consequence-register |
| RouteUsage | Attempt id and run id, route id and version, model version, input and output size, cost, and the gate's result (passed \| rejected, with the schema rule that rejected it). Carries no response content. The record that shows which route served each attempt, for cost and for data residency. | at-least-once: keyed by attempt id; one record per attempt, rejected attempts included | s4.response-proposal-gate | s4.route-usage-receipts |
| CredentialLease | Lease id, the exact grant id it serves, test identity, scope (the target and actions the grant names, nothing more), not-before and expiry, signature and key reference, revocation status. Short-lived and single-purpose; a long-lived secret never crosses into a cell. A restore credential is a lease of this shape issued with an injection grant: its scope is the restore alone, it is sealed to the cell that holds the restore grant, it travels inside that grant, and it is valid until the grant's expiry plus the cell's clock-skew margin. | exactly-once consumption: bound to one grant id; single use; expiry against trusted time; revocation honored before use | s3.credential-lease-broker | s3.bounded-runner, s3.action-control-service, s3.local-enforcement-point |
| DefensiveObservation | Observation id, detection source, observed-at, target and identity seen, the execution receipt it matches or none (unexplained activity), detection and determination times, and the incident reference when one opened. Recorded by the defensive side under its own control, so a test's effect can be reconciled with what defenders saw. | at-least-once: keyed by detection source and observation id; reconciliation appends, never edits the observation | ext.soc-incident-response | s5.evidence-intake-validator |
| ReviewClaim | Claim id, origin (threat model \| security review), service and component, the stated intent or invariant, author role, date, status (unverified \| contradicted \| demonstrated) and the evidence references that contradict or demonstrate it. A claim is what a review says should hold; it is never a fact or coverage. Free text in a claim keeps its untrusted marking. | versioned record: claim id plus version; status changes append with their evidence references | ext.security-review-practice, s1.normative-store | s1.normative-store, s7.tool-yield-evaluator |
| GatedResponse | Attempt id, route and model version, the declared output schema and version, the schema-valid output, and the validation result. The response-proposal gate's output when the result is a classification, a typed answer or a draft instead of a finding, action or patch. Output that came from untrusted input keeps its untrusted marking; passing the schema makes it well formed, never trusted. | at-most-once per attempt: attempt id; keeps the untrusted marking of its input; never retried into a different attempt | s4.response-proposal-gate | s2.quarantined-lead-reader, s2.typed-decision-worker, s2.proof-condition-drafter |
| ApprovalOutcome | ActionSpec hash, or for a clearing of outsider-labeled text the labeled record's id and the fields asked to clear; approver identity, decision (approved \| edited \| rejected; for a clearing, cleared \| kept labeled), the edit when edited, and the time from queue entry to decision. Says nothing about whether the proposal or clearing request was seeded; the sampling service joins the outcome to its own frame. | at-least-once: keyed by ActionSpec hash and approver; one terminal decision per approval request | s3.approval-service, s5.review-console | s7.sampling-service |
| AutonomyGrant | Grant id, the one judgment type it covers, scope, granted_by (the business risk owner) and concurrence (the security risk owner), granted_on, expires_on, the honesty record (what the system reported matches what the control plane recorded) and the correctness record (its verdicts hold on samples it cannot predict, checked by a different means), each with its latest sample result, and status (active \| lapsed \| revoked). Lapses the moment either record slips. Grants the right to learn from the system's own validations for that judgment type only; never authority to act, and the part that proposes a test never judges it. Never wider than the design limit for its judgment type: promotion review refuses a grant that reaches past the limit, and lapses an outstanding grant at once when a new limit version leaves it wider. | versioned record: grant id plus version; lapse and revocation append with the record that caused them; an expired grant is never renewed in place | s7.promotion-review | s5.evidence-intake-validator, s7.evaluation-operations, s5.learning-store, s7.promotion-review |
| KeptTestStatus | Test id and version, weakness class, declared dependencies, purpose (regression of a fixed weakness \| held dependency, naming the held finding and the control the test exercises), owner (the team that answers for the test; for a held-dependency test, the team that owns the control), re-test interval (for a held-dependency test, the longest time allowed between its runs; its time-based runs keep that interval in either lane), lane for its time-based runs (blocking \| scheduled; a run on a change that touches its declared dependencies blocks in either lane), status (active \| quarantined \| retired \| set aside pending repair), last failure, last run, last re-proof (when, against which reverted fix, who ran it, result fail as required \| passed and so no longer failing \| not re-provable (the revert conflicts, or the copy no longer builds or starts) with the fix's age, and for a passed result the verifier's independent confirmation or refutation), and the record of the latest change of lane or status: reason (quiet period passed, false failures, re-proof passed and confirmed, covered by a cheaper check), who decided, and for a quarantine its exception record (accepted by, concurrence, expires) or for a retirement the covering road entry version or derived check id, that check's own kept status and last result, that check's result against this test's reverted-fix copy (it must fail), and the security risk owner's acceptance. A held-dependency test passes while the control stops the path and fails when its run meets the weakness's proof condition, which converts its held finding; its re-proof runs against an isolated copy with the control switched off, where it must meet the proof condition, and it is never retired behind a covering check while its finding is held. A retired test keeps its record and its code; nothing is deleted. | versioned record: test id plus version; every lane or status change appends with who decided it; a retired test is never deleted | s6.retest-regression-planner, s6.regression-catalog, people.security-risk-owner, s5.independent-verifier | s6.regression-catalog, s7.flow-cost-telemetry, s5.disposition-review, s3.gate-failure-policy, s7.value-ledger, s6.kept-test-mirror |
| DetectionProposal | Proposal id, the validated finding and weakness class it comes from, the observable behavior of the demonstrated path (what a defender would see, never the proof condition or an expected-outcome hash), the proposed detection logic in the defensive side's own format, a reference to the execution receipts that show the behavior, and proposed by. A proposal only: the defensive side reviews, tunes, owns and deploys each detection, and reports its outcome back as a defensive observation. | append-only record: keyed by proposal id; the defensive side's review outcome appends and never overwrites | s6.hardening-service | ext.soc-incident-response |
| EscapeRecord | Escape id; the accepted finding it belongs to, with its path key and weakness class. Detection: the source (incident response, bug bounty, external report, production testing, monitoring, the discovery practice or another the organization declares), the detecting role, and detected-at. Attribution: the role that attributed it and the method it used (for example the artifact digest the running system reported, matched to the machine deployment record, or the change history of the affected code or configuration), the deployment that shipped the weakness, and whether detection fell within the organization's detection window after that deployment; a later detection is a late escape, recorded and reported apart from the escape rate. An escape that no deployment shipped, such as configuration drift, a newly disclosed weakness in a dependency that did not change, or a change made outside the pipeline, is recorded with no attributable deployment and reported in a bucket of its own, apart from both escape rates. Confirmation: the confirming role, which differs from every detecting role, from the producer team and from Ratchet decision owner, confirmed-at, and the reproduction showing the weakness reachable through the running system; until it is confirmed the record is unconfirmed and counts in no measure, and once it is older than the organization's alert age for unconfirmed escapes (the detection window when none is set) it alerts the security risk owner. Cost: blast radius as the consequence tier of each asset the demonstrated path reached (as the consequence register held it at detection) and the services affected; time to recover, from detected-at to the fix verified in the running system, open and reported as running until then; and fix scope, class removed when the closure is constraint enforced or one instance when it is path broken, with the closure record it comes from. For an escape whose finding is an exposed secret, time to recover is reported as time to revoke: from detected-at to the revocation check recording the old credential refused. | versioned record: keyed by escape id; confirmation, recovery and fix scope append as they happen, and an unconfirmed record never counts in a measure | s5.disposition-review | s5.finding-ledger, s7.flow-cost-telemetry, s7.evaluation-operations, people.security-risk-owner |
| DesignLimit | Limit id; what it covers (one producer route, or one judgment type for earned autonomy); the highest consequence tier it may touch on a grant alone (standard, critical or crown-jewel); the environments; the action classes (read-only, reversible, state-changing); set by (role, always the security risk owner), set on, the evidence for the last change, signature and version; and the organization's current consequence line as the business risk owner set it, at or above which a person approves every action whatever the limit says. Held in the dial register in the control zone, so no producer can write it. Every standing permission, batch approval, earned-autonomy grant and dial that lets an agent act, or a judgment stand, without a person approving each instance sits inside it, and records the limit version it was checked against. The record never widens a limit: widening one is a recorded turn by the role that owns it. A limit that narrows, a consequence line that drops, or a rise in the tier of an asset a grant reaches makes a new version; every outstanding grant is checked again against it and lapses at once if it is wider, and no part honors a grant checked only against an older version, with one stated exception: a grant inside a replay window bundle already minted, which is verified offline, is honored until the bundle expires, so a bundle's life is its revocation lag, capped by the organization's longest bundle life or by the posture expiry. Versions only increase, and each signed version carries a lease no longer than the dial register's recovery time objective. Readers keep the newest version they hold and never accept a lower one; the register pushes a narrowing to every reader at once and renews leases on a schedule. A reader whose lease lapses unrenewed, because the register or the path to it is down, sends every action that would run on a grant alone to a person, the narrowest behavior, until a current version arrives (F104). | versioned record: limit id plus version; every change appends with the role that made it and its evidence; each grant records the version it was last checked against, and a grant checked only against an older version is not honored, except a grant inside a window bundle already minted, which is honored until the bundle expires (the bundle's stated revocation lag) | s7.dial-register, people.security-risk-owner | s3.policy-decision-point, s3.approval-service, s7.promotion-review, s5.evidence-intake-validator, s7.dial-register, s6.fix-cap-gate, s5.disposition-review |
| ConsequenceLine | The lowest consequence tier (standard, critical or crown-jewel) at which a person approves every agent action and every judgment, whatever any design limit says; set by (role, always the business risk owner), set on, the reason for the last change, signature and version. Held in the dial register beside the design limits and published with each of them. Lowering the line makes a new limit version; raising it is a recorded change by the business risk owner, never a result of the record alone. | versioned record: one current line plus version; every change appends with the business risk owner who made it and the reason; lowering it starts a new limit version | people.risk-acceptance-owner | s7.dial-register |
| AgentActionRecord | Action id; the producer route or judgment type; the design limit and version it ran under; its authority (approved by a person, or grant-only under a named standing permission, batch approval or earned-autonomy grant). Two predictors, written before the action runs: the route's trailing accuracy over the accuracy window the organization records with its design limits (a count of the route's most recent actions, or a number of days, as the organization chooses), with the window it was read over, and the tier its limit allows with the action's class. What it touched (consequence tier of the assets its effects reached, environment, action class). Its accuracy (correct, wrong or unresolved): for an action a person approved, from the independent check; for an action that ran on a grant alone, from the sampled check, so an unsampled grant-only action is unresolved. For a wrong action its cost, measured apart from both predictors: time to recover, and the severity of any incident it caused as the defensive side rates it. One record per agent action, so phase 2 can test which predictor better forecasts what the agent's errors cost. | append-only record: keyed by action id; the accuracy verdict and the cost of a wrong action append later and never overwrite what the action touched | s3.protected-audit, s5.independent-verifier, ext.soc-incident-response | s7.flow-cost-telemetry |
| FixPath | What security and the owning engineer co-create with an agent before a validated finding enters an owning team's queue. Finding id and path key, owning team, date validated, prepared by (security and the owning engineer, with the agent route and the shared pull request reference), the security side's hours to prepare it, the reproducing test (a test drawn from the replay of the demonstrated path that fails on the current build, or, where no build-level test can reproduce the weakness, a reproduction through the running system in a test environment; with its run reference), the drafted fix (a candidate change or policy change as a reviewed proposal, never merged by its author, or "no draft possible" with the reason recorded), the owning team's decision on the drafted fix (taken, edited, or declined with a reason; a declined draft leaves the team free to write its own fix), and the owned gate (the regression test, or the repeated reproduction through the running system, that the fix must pass, its named owner and the pipeline stage or scheduled check it runs in). Complete when the reproducing test and the owned gate are present and the drafted fix is present or recorded as not possible with its reason. A path completed with no draft possible goes to the owning team's lead and the security risk owner. The owned instance regression is component-local, visible to coding agents and owned by the owning team in its suite. Its version and team suite reference are recorded as InstanceRegression. The protected RegressionSpec is independent aggregate validation and never enters that pull request. A variant opens a separate finding, never an unannounced edit to the pull request. | versioned record: keyed by finding id; the owning team's decision on the drafted fix appends and never overwrites; complete only with all three parts | s6.owner-change-connector, s6.hardening-service | s6.fix-cap-gate, people.engineering-owners, people.security-risk-owner, s7.flow-cost-telemetry |
| FixCapRefusal | Team, finding id and path key, reason, date, kind (refused, or entered over the cap, with the ground: at or above the consequence line, matched by a sourced exploitation signal with the signal's reference and the exploited window it started, an external disclosure deadline with its date, entered while the gate was down with the pass ground recorded at disposition and the result of the gate's check of it on recovery, or sent by the security risk owner), the cap in force (a team cap with the security role that agreed it, or the default cap with the role that set it), the team's open validated findings at the time, and the reviews of a refusal: each review's date, the owning team's reviewer, the co-signature of the security role that agreed the cap, and the outcome (queued, held to the next review, cap revised, or class sent to the paved road); after two consecutive held outcomes, the security risk owner's decision: sent over the cap, or a risk acceptance with its expiry. | append-only record: keyed by finding id and refusal date; each review appends its outcome; a refusal is never deleted | s6.fix-cap-gate, people.engineering-owners, people.security-risk-owner | s6.fix-cap-refusal-log, s6.fix-cap-gate, people.security-risk-owner, s7.flow-cost-telemetry |
| InjectionRecord | Experiment id; the weakness class and the verified fix it reintroduces; the detection layer expected to catch the class (workstation, pull request, pipeline, deployed test environment or production) and the check or watch at that layer; the environment, and for the deployed test environment and production layers the notice mode in force (announced, trusted agent or unannounced) with the trusted agent's role; the service owner's cost estimate and time limit, recorded before the start; the injection grant, the restore approved inside it and its expiry (the time limit, and for production no later than the exposure window the profile sets); the restore grant and the cell that held it; for a pull request, the draft or isolated branch and the status check that blocks its merge; at the workstation layer, the harness that stamped the start and finish; injected-at. Outcome: whether the expected layer caught it, which layer and source caught it when another one did, or that nothing did within the time limit; detected-at and the time to detection; past deployment, the time to recovery, from detection to the fix restored and verified again in the running environment, and before deployment the time to recovery recorded as not applicable, because nothing was merged or deployed; any refused merge attempt; whether the exercise was stopped, by whom, why and by which path (the action control service or the service owner's rollback), and the time from the stop to the environment restored against the organization's ceiling; whether the grant expired and the restore ran at expiry; what ran the restore (the cell's timer, a stop through the action control service, or the service owner's rollback) and whether the control plane was reachable then; the environment restored; and the actual cost beside the estimate. A miss at the expected layer is recorded as a detection gap for that weakness class and layer. | append-only record: keyed by experiment id; detection, a stop, recovery and the actual cost append as they happen and never overwrite the layer and estimate recorded before the start | s6.retest-regression-planner, ext.soc-incident-response, s3.action-control-service, s3.local-enforcement-point | s6.retest-regression-planner, s3.action-control-service, s7.flow-cost-telemetry, s7.evaluation-operations, s3.local-enforcement-point |
| SecretRevocation | For a finding of kind exposed secret. Finding id; the credential's issuer, type and identifier and a fingerprint of its value taken at intake by the exposed-secret intake, never the value itself; where it was exposed (code, history, logs, tickets or another store) with the scan rule and rule version that found it there and the earliest exposure found (first commit, log line or ticket); detected-at; the owning team that holds the credential. Its fix path: the scan rule's hit where the secret was exposed is the reproducing test, the drafted fix is a request to revoke and rotate the credential naming the services that use it, and the owned gate is the scan rule that blocks the secret's return, matching its fingerprint, with its owner and the stages where it runs, kept in the regression catalog. The fix, done by the owning team in its own systems and recorded through its fix path: revoked-at, rotated-at, the services moved to the new credential, and each derived session or token the type's recipe names with how it was ended (revoked, or expired at a stated time); no agent revokes, rotates or holds the credential. Removing the secret from code, history, logs or tickets is recorded beside the fix and never counts as the fix while the old credential still authenticates. The revocation check follows the type's verification recipe (CredentialRecipe), planned by the retest planner as an ActionSpec under the normal authorization envelope, run by the bounded runner and judged by the replay verdict check with no model: first the issuer's status by identifier, where the issuer offers it; otherwise the status probe, announced to the detection side before it runs and repeated over the issuer's propagation time from a fixed egress, with refused counting only for the codes the recipe maps to revoked or invalid. Each attempt's receipt and result (revoked, refused, accepted or unknown), signed by the replay verdict check. A credential the issuer cannot revoke before expiry is recorded as such, and its expiry is the time it fails. A type with no recipe closes on the owning team's attestation of revocation, labeled attested in every record and measure and never read as check-confirmed. Credential failed at: the issuer's revoked status, the first attempt of a series refused on every attempt, the recorded expiry, or the attestation. The exposure-use review, by the detection side: the window from the earliest exposure to credential failed at, the issuer logs read with the stretch each one covers, and the result for each stretch (no use, use attributable to the owner, unexplained use, or not verifiable, for a stretch no issuer log covers, recorded with its start and end dates); unexplained use opens an incident and blocks closure. A finding with a not-verifiable stretch closes only when the security risk owner accepts the residual risk of that stretch, recorded with the owner's name, the date and the stretches accepted; the acceptance never stands in for the revocation check, which still proves the credential failed. Time to revoke: from detected-at to credential failed at, open and reported as running until then. | append-only record: keyed by finding id; the owning team's revocation and rotation and each revocation check result append as they happen; the record holds the credential's fingerprint and never its value | s5.exposed-secret-intake, s3.replay-verdict, s6.owner-change-connector, s5.evidence-intake-validator, s6.change-closure-records, ext.soc-incident-response, people.security-risk-owner | s5.evidence-intake-validator, s6.change-closure-records, s6.retest-regression-planner, ext.soc-incident-response, s7.flow-cost-telemetry |
| LearnedItem | Item id and version, judgment type, the evidence partition (jurisdiction and business unit) of the findings it came from, what was learned (a worked reproduction, a verdict, a hypothesis that held or failed) with the finding ids it came from, and its provenance: confirmed by a person (who and when) or self-validated under an earned-autonomy grant (grant id and version), the ids of its inputs, and its outsider label set (OutsiderLabelSet): each outsider label on its inputs with its clearing record (cleared by a person, or by a model-free check with its version; when; the fields cleared). Signed by disposition review when it is stored, under the partition's key. The store recomputes the set from the input ids when the item is written and at each retrieval. At retrieval: released or withheld, the reason when withheld (no provenance, outsider label not cleared, recorded labels do not cover the inputs' labels, grant lapsed, grant status unreadable), and whether a withheld item traced to an untagged stored item. An item with no clearing record keeps the label. | append-only record: keyed by item id and version; provenance and clearing records append and never change; each retrieval decision is logged | s5.disposition-review, s5.learning-store | s5.learning-store, s1.context-compiler, s7.flow-cost-telemetry |
| OutsiderLabelSet | The outsider labels on one record, field by field. For each labeled field: its path in the record, the outsider sources that reached it (a lead, a bug bounty report, a target's response recorded in a receipt, or an earlier labeled record, each by id), and its clearing record when it has one (the person, or the model-free check and its version, and the time). A part outside the producer computes it, and a producer never declares it: the context compiler for each context item from its source records, the request-data gate for each model request as the union over everything the run was given and has received, and evidence intake (or the independent verifier, for an artifact taken from the artifact exchange) for what a run produced, from the gate's records for that run. Evidence relationships carry it field by field: a part clears only the fields it constrains to a closed set or resolves against a store, and every other field keeps its labels. A field whose labels cannot be computed counts as labeled. Carried by context items (ContextSnapshot), ModelRequest, ModelResponse, CandidateFinding, ValidationArtifact, PatchCandidate, ExecutionReceipt, FindingDisposition, FixBrief and LearnedItem. | versioned record: carried inside the record it labels; a clearing appends a clearing record to the field and never removes the field's sources; recomputed from input ids where a part checks coverage | s1.context-compiler, s4.request-data-gate, s5.evidence-intake-validator, s5.independent-verifier, s5.review-console | s5.evidence-intake-validator, s5.independent-verifier, s5.disposition-review, s5.fix-brief-builder, s5.learning-store, s7.flow-cost-telemetry |
| CredentialRecipe | A verification recipe for one credential type, kept in the regression catalog with its owner. The issuer and how to reach it; the status query by identifier when the issuer offers one (it needs neither the value nor its use); otherwise the status probe: the endpoint, the issuer's response codes that mean revoked or invalid, those that mean valid but forbidden, the issuer's stated propagation time, how many attempts over that time, and the fixed egress the probe runs from. The derived sessions and tokens the type can mint (temporary credentials, tokens from a refresh token or signing key) and how each is ended. Whether the type can be revoked before it expires. A credential type with no recipe closes on the owning team's attestation, labeled attested. | versioned register: keyed by credential type; a recipe change is a new version and the next revocation check uses it; a type with no recipe closes only on attestation | s6.regression-catalog | s5.exposed-secret-intake, s6.retest-regression-planner |
| SealedCredential | An exposed credential's value sealed under the owning team's key in key management, created only when its type's recipe needs a status probe. The finding id, the fingerprint it must match, sealed-at, the probe series it may be released for, released-at, and destroyed-at, which follows the series' last attempt. No record, lead or model request holds it, and it is released only to the credential lease broker. | sealed record: released once per probe series to the lease broker and destroyed after its last attempt; an unreleased value is destroyed when the finding closes | s5.exposed-secret-intake, s3.key-management | s3.key-management, s3.credential-lease-broker |
| ConformanceRead | For a finding of kind infrastructure misconfiguration. Finding id; the resources and the environment; the declared intent they must match (the policy or configuration rule, its owner and version); the live configuration state read through the estate's own interfaces with no model, read-at, and the result (match, mismatch or unknown). The kept gate: a policy check on the infrastructure code that blocks the change returning, or drift detection on the live state, kept in the regression catalog with its owner. Detected-at and conformed-at (the first read that matched), from which time to conform is computed, running until a read matches. | append-only record: keyed by finding id; every read appends with its result; unknown never closes | s6.release-state-correlator | s6.change-closure-records, s7.flow-cost-telemetry |
| RestoreGrant | Restore grant id; the injection grant and experiment it belongs to; the restore it authorizes (the exact undo of the injected change, bound by hash to the ActionSpec that carried both), the environment and target; the expiry, equal to the injection grant's; the cell that holds it; the restore credential, a credential lease the lease broker issued with the injection grant, scoped to the restore's target and effect, sealed to that cell, valid until the expiry plus the cell's clock-skew margin and consumed with the grant; signature and key reference. Issued with the injection grant and delivered to the local enforcement point of the cell that applies the injection before the injection starts; verified there offline against cached key references and consumed once, by the cell's dead-man timer at expiry or early on a stop. The runner runs the restore under the restore credential it holds, with no lease request. Nothing can extend it: a longer injection needs a new grant. | exactly-once consumption: signed artifact with the injection grant's expiry; verified offline by the local enforcement point and consumed once, by the cell's timer at expiry or early on a stop; never extended | s3.action-control-service | s3.local-enforcement-point, s3.bounded-runner |
| ExploitationSignal | A sourced attacker-side fact that a weakness is being exploited: the source (a known exploited vulnerability catalog, or observed exploitation reported by the defensive side or a threat intelligence provider), the catalog entry or report reference, the vulnerability identifier, the component as the source names it (vendor or project, and product), the affected versions as a version range with the scheme it is written in (empty when no source states them), the record the range was read from, and the date listed or observed. The public catalog states no versions, so a catalog listing carries a range only when a vulnerability database record for the same identifier supplies one. Structured fields only; free threat text stays with the source adapters as untrusted text and never crosses. The fix-cap gate matches a signal to a finding by rule: on the vulnerability identifier, or on the component when the version the finding's weakness sits in falls inside the signal's range. A signal with an empty range matches on its identifier alone, never on the component. A signal never creates a finding, never lowers a posture and never counts as evidence. | versioned record: keyed by source and catalog entry or report reference; a later listing or retraction appends a version and never overwrites the date first listed or observed | ext.known-exploited-catalog, ext.threat-intelligence | s6.fix-cap-gate, s5.disposition-review |
| PriorityInputs | Recorded at disposition for each accepted finding, as ordering inputs inside a consequence tier: finding id and path key; exposure of the demonstrated path's entry point (reachable from the internet, internal, or unknown), read from the scope and the source facts with no model; and automatable (yes or no), set by the independent reviewer from the reproduction: yes when every step of the reproduction ran with no person's input. Each value records who or which check set it and when. An unknown or unrecorded value orders as if it were the more urgent one, so a missing answer never moves a finding later. Neither input lowers a posture, moves a finding to a lower tier or counts as evidence. Pass grounds: disposition review also records, by rule and with no model, which of the fix cap's pass conditions hold for the finding (a reached asset at or above the consequence line, a match in the known exploited catalog copy or a sourced observed exploitation signal on its vulnerability identifier or its component and version, or an external disclosure deadline), each with the input it was read from, and records them again whenever a new catalog entry, signal, deadline or line change touches an open finding. The fix-cap gate does not depend on them while it is up; while it is down, they are the only grounds on which a finding enters an owning team's queue. | versioned record: keyed by finding id; a changed exposure or automatable value appends a version with who or which check set it and never overwrites the value in force at disposition | s5.disposition-review | s6.fix-cap-gate, s7.flow-cost-telemetry, s6.owner-change-connector |
| MitigationRecord | An interim mitigation on a validated finding, recorded by the security risk owner while the permanent fix is assembled: finding id and path key; what holds the path (for example a scoped firewall rule, a disabled feature or a revoked permission); who applied it and when; its expiry; and a replay of the demonstrated path with the mitigation in place, planned by the retest planner under the normal authorization envelope and judged by the replay verdict check with no model (path broken, open or unknown), with its receipt. The mitigation counts as holding only while its last replay says path broken and it has not expired. It never closes the finding: the finding stays open in the ledger, keeps its place in the fix path and the queue, and its time to remediate keeps running. A replay that finds the path open, an expiry, or a removal ends the mitigation and alerts the security risk owner. | append-only record: keyed by finding id and mitigation; each replay result, expiry and removal appends as it happens and never overwrites what held the path or who applied it | people.security-risk-owner, s3.replay-verdict, s5.evidence-intake-validator | s6.change-closure-records, s7.flow-cost-telemetry, people.security-risk-owner, s5.evidence-intake-validator |
| DisclosureRecord | Disclosure in either direction for one finding. Inbound: a weakness reported from outside the organization, with the reporter's reference and the external deadline the reporter or the coordination policy sets for a fix or publication. Outbound: a weakness in a third-party component reported to its maintainers by the role that owns outbound reports, under the coordination policy the profile names, with the date reported, the external deadline agreed, and the outcome with its date (acknowledged, fixed upstream, published, or declined with a reason). A finding with an external deadline passes the fix cap, and the security risk owner is alerted ahead of the deadline by the profile's lead time, and when it is recorded where no lead time is set. A disclosure record never closes a finding and never counts as evidence of a fix. | append-only record: keyed by finding id and direction; a changed deadline or a new outcome appends with its date and never overwrites the deadline or outcome recorded before it | people.security-risk-owner | s6.fix-cap-gate, s5.disposition-review, s6.change-closure-records, s7.flow-cost-telemetry, people.security-risk-owner |
| ReleaseAttestation | One release of Ratchet's own software, a runner or cell image, or a self-hosted model weights file: artifact kind, name and version, artifact digest; build provenance at SLSA Build Level 3 (the hosted, isolated build platform's identity, the source repository and revision, the build definition and its parameters, the dependencies resolved with their digests), generated and signed by the build platform and never by the build steps; a software bill of materials; the release signature with its key reference; the security risk owner's approval when the release widens what can run; and rollout stage and rollback reference. Ratchet's built parts follow the organization's own secure development requirements; the attestation records the requirement set each build was checked against. An artifact with no attestation, a signature that does not verify against a current key reference whose purpose is release, provenance below Build Level 3, or a digest that differs from the attested one is never started or served. The local enforcement point checks this before anything starts in a cell, and the route registry before it pins a weights digest; the model gateway then serves only weights whose loaded digest matches that pin. Each release attestation carries a monotonically increasing release sequence number covered by the release signature. Release signatures and rollback authorizations verify only against a current key reference whose purpose is release. For release admission a cell is a stable logical execution destination, independent of its disposable image or enforcement process. The local enforcement point keeps that cell's highest verified release sequence, last consumed rollback number and sticky refusal ranges in durable storage outside the disposable cell; all survive restart, rebuild and restore. A new or rebuilt cell takes its first mark and rollback state from the release pipeline through authenticated provisioning, never from the first arriving release or an empty local store; unavailable or stale initialization refuses all starts. A rollback authorization is part of the release attestation and is signed by key management for the pipeline with purpose release; it names the cell, its highest verified sequence, the running sequence being rolled away from, the older target sequence and digest, a per-cell rollback number and the highest release sequence issued before this rollback. The enforcement point requires the target to be older than the running release and the rollback number to exceed the last consumed number, and atomically persists its consumption and the new refusal range before starting the target. A rollback never lowers the high-water mark and permanently refuses every previously issued release from its rolled-away-from sequence through its signed issued-through sequence, even with another rollback authorization. These refusal ranges accumulate; reusing that code at or above a rolled-away-from sequence requires the pipeline to issue a fresh release with a new sequence beyond the applicable refusal ranges and the high-water mark. Outside these ranges an older release still requires a matching unused rollback authorization; a missing, invalid, reused or non-increasing authorization is refused before any start. | versioned artifact: artifact digest plus signed increasing release sequence; release-purpose verification before admission; durable per-cell high-water mark, consumed rollback number and sticky refusal ranges; pipeline provisioning initializes new and rebuilt cells | s3.policy-release-pipeline | s3.local-enforcement-point, s4.qualification-route-registry, s3.protected-audit |
| PostmortemRecord | Postmortem id, the trigger that opened it (an effect outside an approved scope or with no approval; a closure contradicted by a later confirmed escape on its path key; evidence lost or unreadable; one incident using more than the share of an objective's error budget that Ratchet's operating policy sets; the loop canary's dead-man alarm; a seeded proposal or seeded change that reached execution or merged; or a request from any role), references to the records the trigger came from, owner (the on-call role of the first failing part), timeline, contributing causes stated as conditions of the system and never as a person, action items each recorded as a design change, a profile change or a work unit with its owner, the security risk owner's signed reading, and status (open, reviewed, closed when every action item has landed or been declined with a reason). | versioned record: keyed by postmortem id; the review, the security risk owner's signature and each action item's outcome append; a record is never deleted, and one trigger event opens at most one postmortem | s7.reliability-monitor | people.security-risk-owner, people.control-plane-operator |
| GateCatchRate | Gate (approval, clearing of outsider text, proof condition, or decision on a finding or closure), the person it is for, window, seeded items received, seeded items caught (rejected or edited at the approval gate, kept labeled at the clearing gate, refused at the proof-condition and decider gates), the catch rate with its interval and sample size, the gate's floor, and whether a breach is open. Computed by rule from the recorded outcomes. Never names which items were seeds. Restricted to its listed consumers: each person receives only their own rate, and the seeding owner, the role that runs the sampling and seeding service outside the gate's reporting line, reads every person's rate where the service computes it. Sponsors and managers see pooled rates. | latest-wins record: keyed by gate, person and window; recomputed by rule from recorded outcomes, so a resend replaces the window's value; a window with no record reads as silent, never as zero | s7.sampling-service | people.accountable-authorities, people.scenario-owner, people.independent-security-staff |
| OutcomeFeedback | Receiving role, decision type (refusals over a fix cap, ship-through acceptances and earned-autonomy grants, design limits and floors, kept tests and detections proposed), measure name and definition, window, value with its interval or sample size, and references to the records it is computed from (escape records, agent action records, kept-test status, fix paths). Computed by rule from recorded outcomes; carries no free text from a finding. A measure with too few outcomes in the window reads too few to call, never zero. | latest-wins record: keyed by receiving role, decision type, measure and window; recomputed by rule from the records it references, so a resend replaces the window's value; a window with no record reads as silent, never as zero | s7.flow-cost-telemetry, s6.regression-catalog | people.engineering-owners, people.risk-acceptance-owner, people.security-risk-owner, people.purple-team |
| VerifiedObservation | What the independent verifier observed when it reproduced a result, or what the evidence intake validator observed when it validated an intake: the run or intake id, the proof condition's id and version, the verdict and assertion results or the validation outcome, the tests, telemetry and execution receipts it rests on as references and hashes, the verifier's or validator's version, and the time. Evidence content: dispositions, closures and audits read it back, so it lands in a store partitioned by the profile's partition keys. Carries the outsider label set of its inputs field by field, less the fields the verifier or validator constrains (the verdict, status classes, assertion results, references and hashes). | append-only record: keyed by run or intake id and the observer's version; a repeat with the same key and hashes adds nothing, and a new observation appends and never overwrites an earlier one | s5.independent-verifier, s5.evidence-intake-validator | s5.protected-evidence-store |
| ClassTestResult | The full result of one class test: a rule at one version run against each reproduced member of its proven class at the member's revision before the fix, with each member's finding id and revision, whether the rule caught it, missed it or could not run against it, where the rule fired and what it reported. Kept in the regression catalog beside the kept regressions, appended to the rule version it tested and never overwritten. It never reaches a part where coding agents run: member locations and the rule's output on them would show an agent where reproduced weaknesses sit and how a rule sees them. Only pass or fail per rule version reaches agent-reachable parts; a separate per-class totals projection reaches the policy-owning team: people, never an agent directly. | append-only record: keyed by rule id, rule version and test date; appended to the rule version it tested and never overwrites an earlier result; it never leaves the regression catalog | s6.regression-catalog, s6.retest-regression-planner | s6.regression-catalog |
| RegressionHashes | For each regression test declared for the next replay window, the test id and version and its registered expected-outcome hash, as the regression catalog sends them to the credential lease broker for the window bundle. Never carries the expected boundary behavior, the observation projection or any other part of the test. | versioned record: keyed by test id and version; carries no expected behavior, only the registered hash | s6.regression-catalog | s3.credential-lease-broker |
| WeaknessStatement | The weakness a signed proof condition names, as the fixture and oracle registry sends it to the fix brief builder: the proof condition's id and version, the weakness class, its location, the boundary that failed, and the signature reference. Never carries the expected effect, the hidden expected value or assertion, negative controls, legitimate behavior checks, or anything that would show the weakness is not real. | immutable artifact by version: keyed by proof condition id and version; a new condition version yields a new statement, never an edit in place | s5.fixture-oracle-registry | s5.fix-brief-builder |
| DiscoveryLeadSubmission | A finding entered through operator intake from the organization's discovery practice, with the originating source, tool and rule versions when available, target, location, raw description and source references. Its text stays untrusted. Tool signal intake normalizes it into ToolLead before storing it. It carries no exposed credential value, which goes only to exposed-secret intake. | at-least-once: Keyed by request or finding reference, author and time; each decision appends a record. | s2.operator-interface | s1.tool-signal-intake |
| AutonomyGrantRequest | The business risk owner requests earned autonomy for one judgment type: scope, decision, author, time and expiry, with references to the honesty and correctness records. Promotion review checks the current design limit before registering an AutonomyGrant. | at-least-once: Keyed by request or finding reference, author and time; each decision appends a record. | people.risk-acceptance-owner | s7.promotion-review |
| AutonomyGrantConcurrence | The security risk owner concurs on a requested earned-autonomy grant: request reference, scope, concurrence, author and time. Promotion review checks the current design limit and records this concurrence in the issued AutonomyGrant. | at-least-once: Keyed by request or finding reference, author and time; each decision appends a record. | people.security-risk-owner | s7.promotion-review |
| AdminSessionRequest | A request for an administrative session: requester identity, target part, permitted operations, requested duration and break-glass reason. The broker issues the session only after the required approvals. Before acting on this input, the broker durably appends its request reference, authenticated sender identity, received time, target and scope, payload hash and acceptance or refusal with its reason to protected audit under custody neither the requester nor the approver can alter. A refusal is recorded even when no session is issued. If the append fails, the broker refuses the input and opens no session. Retries retain the original request reference and append an attempt, never overwrite a decision. | at-least-once: Keyed by request or finding reference, author and time; each decision appends a record. | people.control-plane-operator | s3.privileged-access-broker, s3.protected-audit |
| AdminSessionApproval | Security risk owner approval of a break-glass session request: request reference, approver identity, decision, scope and time. It is an input to the broker, which records and issues the administrative session. Before acting on this input, the broker durably appends its request reference, authenticated sender identity, received time, target and scope, payload hash and acceptance or refusal with its reason to protected audit under custody neither the requester nor the approver can alter. A refusal is recorded even when no session is issued. If the append fails, the broker refuses the input and opens no session. Retries retain the original request reference and append an attempt, never overwrite a decision. | at-least-once: Keyed by request or finding reference, author and time; each decision appends a record. | people.security-risk-owner | s3.privileged-access-broker, s3.protected-audit |
| PolicyChangeProposal | A proposed policy, allowlist, route-table or schema change as code: target, base version, proposed diff, author and reason. The release pipeline tests and signs the resulting policy bundle. | at-least-once: Keyed by request or finding reference, author and time; each decision appends a record. | people.control-plane-operator | s3.policy-release-pipeline |
| PolicyChangeApproval | Approval of a policy change whose deny-diff widens what may run: proposed change reference, deny-diff reference, approver identity, decision and time. The release pipeline includes this approval in the signed bundle. | at-least-once: Keyed by request or finding reference, author and time; each decision appends a record. | people.security-risk-owner | s3.policy-release-pipeline |
| PersonWrittenBrief | A person-written weakness description submitted to the fix brief builder with the finding reference, author and time. It carries no outsider text, model output, proof condition, expected outcome or kept regression. The builder validates the submission and signs the resulting FixBrief for its author before sending it to a fixing agent. | at-least-once: Keyed by request or finding reference, author and time; each decision appends a record. | people.independent-security-staff | s5.fix-brief-builder |
| InstanceRegression | A component-local regression co-created by security, the owning engineer and an agent in one correction pull request. Test id and version, finding and weakness class, owning team, repository and suite, test source revision, declared dependencies, local input and assertions, failing-before and passing-after run references, and the reviewed pull request reference. The test is visible to coding agents and owned by the team. It contains no aggregate proof condition, chained-path validation, protected fixture or aggregate expected outcome. A later variant enters as a new finding. Only normal owning-team review changes this test. | at-least-once: Append-only observations keyed by test id, version, source revision and run. | s6.owner-change-connector | s6.team-regression-suite, s6.kept-test-mirror |
| InstanceTestObservation | An automatically collected read-only observation from the team repository and CI interface. Test id and version, suite and owning team, repository revision, dependency scope and configuration, run id, observed-at and source reference, enabled or skipped state, result passing, failing or unknown, collection success or unavailable, and any reviewed retirement reference. A complete expected inventory is reconciled against the observed manifest so a removed test is detectable. No source credential can write the repository or CI. An absent run or collection failure never counts as a pass. | at-least-once: Append-only observations keyed by test id, version, source revision and run. | s6.team-regression-suite | s6.kept-test-mirror |
| KeptTestMirrorStatus | Security's read-only inventory evidence, keyed by test id and version, finding, weakness class, owning team, suite, source revision, dependency scope, configuration, observation time and run reference. Records expected enrollment and each observation, removed, skipped or failing drift with its normal-loop finding reference, unknown or stale collection, and any authorized retirement. Near-JIT risk review states its assessment revision, configuration, scope, as-of time and freshness window. Protection is current only with an enabled passing observation matching all of them. Instance, aggregate and class evidence stay distinct. This is evidence for a person's risk assessment, never an authorization or a closure verdict. Carries metadata only, no protected test input, proof condition, expected outcome or chained-path details. | at-least-once: Append-only observations keyed by test id, version, source revision and run. | s6.kept-test-mirror | people.security-risk-owner, s7.flow-cost-telemetry, s6.retest-regression-planner |
| jig:HunkRouteAdvice | A model's advice to put hunks on the human route. It can only add: it names hunks and a reason, and carries no way to lower a route. | at-least-once: keyed by change id, hunk id and advisor version; a repeat adds nothing, since advice can only add a hunk to the human route | jig:hunk-risk-advisor | jig:hunk-router |
| jig:CreationOutcome | What a developer's sessions produced, as it turned out. Shown only to that developer; managers see pooled rates. A window with too few merged changes reads too few to call. | latest-wins record: keyed by author, measure and closed cohort; recomputed by rule from the shared record, so a resend replaces the cohort's value; a window with no record reads as silent, never as zero | jig:measurement-plane | jig:people.creation-developer |
| jig:ReviewerDrift | How a reviewer's decisions on agent-written changes are drifting. Reported by a team other than the reviewers' own; which changes were seeds is never shown to a reviewer. Only the measurement plane writes it; the seeded catches in it come from Ratchet's evaluation as a record of their own. | latest-wins record: keyed by reviewer and window; recomputed by rule from review outcomes and the seeded catch counts Ratchet sends, so a resend replaces the window's value; a window with no record reads as silent, never as zero | jig:measurement-plane | jig:people.change-reviewer, jig:people.high-risk-reviewer |
| jig:LayerCatchRate | How well each layer and each rule catches what it is meant to catch. The layer that should have caught each defect is assigned outside the loop. Class tests travel separately as pass or fail per rule version; per-class totals never enter this plane. | latest-wins record: keyed by layer or rule and closed cohort; recomputed by rule from the layer log and the assigned catch layers, so a resend replaces the cohort's value; a window with no record reads as silent, never as zero | jig:measurement-plane | jig:people.creation-policy-owner |
| shared:RoadEntry | One paved-road entry at one version, with how deployed state proves its use and the conformance check that reads it. Each escape hatch is named and logged when used. | versioned artifact: entry id plus version; released through the platform pipeline | s6.paved-road-catalog | s2.request-dispatcher, s6.retest-regression-planner, s6.regression-catalog, s6.road-conformance-check, jig:context-plane, s5.road-assurance-record |
| shared:DerivedCheck | The only form in which a kept regression reaches a part where coding agents run; the kept regression itself stays in the loop. Static or in-process only: a runtime replay never yields a derived check. Its pattern is the body the check suite compiles into configuration coding agents can read, so it holds a general pattern for the weakness class the kept test holds, never the kept test's input, its fixtures or the boundary behavior it expects. The fixed message is set when the check is derived and never states the expected boundary behavior or an expected outcome. Carries no expected-outcome hash, no proof condition and no text an outsider wrote. | versioned record: keyed by check id and version with the kept regression's test id and version it came from; a new regression version yields a new check version, never an edit in place | s6.regression-catalog | jig:check-suite |
| shared:DroppedCheck | One derived check the secure-creation loop dropped or disabled, logged so it counts against the catch layer instead of disappearing. | append-only record: keyed by check id, version and time; a re-enabled check appends a new record | jig:check-suite, jig:measurement-plane | s1.tool-signal-intake, jig:measurement-plane |
| shared:ProvenClass | The projection of a failure class for the secure-creation loop. Only classes with at least one independently reproduced member. It names the class, its definition and fix rule, and the road entries and derived checks that answer it. Its definition names no service, component, path or file of the organization's own. While any member finding is open, the projection shows the class's existence, definition and fix rule, with no member count, services or components spanned, or highest consequence tier. A third-party class names the component without its versions until a fix exists. Per-class member counts are never carried in this projection, including after every member closes; they go only to the policy-owning team: people, never an agent, never to an agent-reachable part or the model route. Spans and highest consequence tier are sent only once every member's finding is closed; a class that gains an open member again sends its next version without them, and the loop keeps only the current version of each class. The model provider is a reader through the coding agent's model route, so the same limits apply to what reaches it. Carries no verdict reference, fixture-set reference, kept-regression id or finding text; a derived check names the kept test it came from by id, and the test itself never crosses. The policy-owning team turns each into a rule. A class's projection version increases when its definition, fix rule, fix availability, road claims, derived-check references, weakness identifiers or disclosed spans or consequence tier change, or when it enters or leaves the all-members-closed state; membership changes while already open do not by themselves increase that version. | versioned record: class id plus projection version; public semantic or closure-state changes increase it; membership changes while already open alone do not | s5.failure-class-registry | jig:context-plane, jig:people.creation-policy-owner |
| shared:RoadAssuranceStatus | The projection of road assurance for the secure-creation loop. Carries no fixture-set or verdict reference and no road-level regression result. For each entry version its status is current or not current, with no class outcomes and no reason for a status change, including a failed vetting or a withdrawal after a leak. Its vetted scope and known limits use API and configuration identifiers and named pattern classes, never an input shape or sequence. They never hold a test's input, fixture content, expected outcome or a bypass description. | versioned record: entry id plus version, projected from the road assurance record; superseded or withdrawn, never overwritten | s5.road-assurance-record | jig:context-plane |
| shared:FixBrief | The only form in which a description of a weakness reaches a fixing agent or a fix drafter. Written by a person, or derived by the fix brief builder only from the weakness a signed proof condition names and the model-free reproduction. Signed when it is made, by the builder or for the person who wrote it, so a brief changed on its way to a fixing agent no longer verifies. A derived brief's outsider label set is empty and covers its inputs' labels, checked when the brief is issued; a brief that needs free-text steps, the target's output or any other labeled field is written by a person. Carries no outsider text, no output of a model that read outsider text, no expected outcome, no expected-outcome hash, nothing that says what would show the weakness is not real, and no kept regression. | versioned record: keyed by finding id; a new reproduction or proof-condition version yields a new brief version, never an edit in place; how it was made is part of the record | s5.fix-brief-builder | people.engineering-owners, jig:coding-agent, s6.owner-change-connector, s6.hardening-service, s7.flow-cost-telemetry, s5.fix-brief-builder |
| shared:CreationRule | A rule the policy-owning team turned from a proven class, with its body as the owner published it, sent out for the test that shows it catches the class: a run against the class's reproduced members at their revisions before the fix, whose full result Ratchet's regression catalog keeps. Only the policy-owning team writes it; the test's result comes back as a record of its own, pass or fail per rule version. A rule with no passing class test steers and reports; it never gates. | versioned artifact: rule id plus version, written only by the policy-owning team; a class test's result is a record of its own and never edits the rule | jig:people.creation-policy-owner | jig:check-suite, s6.regression-catalog, s6.retest-regression-planner |
| shared:CatchLayer | The layer that should have caught one accepted defect, assigned by Ratchet's evaluation group, independent of the secure-creation loop and of the road owner. | versioned record: keyed by finding id; a reassignment appends a version with who assigned it and never overwrites the layer in force when a measure was reported | s7.tool-yield-evaluator | jig:measurement-plane |
| shared:ClassTestCount | The binary projection of a class test for agent-reachable parts: only rule id, rule version and pass or fail. It carries no class id or class version, per-class counts, member identities or locations, test output or timing. A class test passes only when it caught every member it ran against and ran against at least one; any miss or zero tested is fail, and not-run members do not count as tested. The catalog computes this result from its protected full result. A rule gates only after a pass at its current version; otherwise it steers and reports. Per-class counts go only to the policy-owning team: people, never an agent through the separate direct totals projection, never to an agent-reachable part or the model route. | latest-wins record: rule id plus rule version; only the current pass or fail is projected from the protected class-test result | s6.regression-catalog | jig:check-suite, jig:measurement-plane, jig:people.creation-policy-owner |
| shared:CreationLead | A lead from the secure-creation loop's checks and AI reviewers, with the tool, rule and version and the layer that raised it; a rule-summary lead stands for a high-volume rule, with a random sample of its hits. The loop's own triage verdict travels as a label, never as a filter. Only the loop's measurement plane writes it, and the tool signal intake normalizes each into a tool lead, which is Ratchet's own record. Text a tool or an AI reviewer wrote can quote outsiders, so those fields are marked untrusted. | at-least-once: keyed by tool, rule id (with rename mapping) and fingerprint; the tool signal intake normalizes each into a tool lead and deduplicates within the tool | jig:measurement-plane | s1.tool-signal-intake |
| shared:ProvenanceStamp | The provenance stamp the secure-creation loop attaches to one change: the person whose session produced it, the tool and the model with their versions, the session and the skill-bundle hash, as the harness's session telemetry declares them. Signed when the change leaves the session, so a stamp changed on its way no longer verifies against the key the organization registered for the stamper. Only the provenance stamper writes it; a change from a harness that exports no telemetry carries no stamp. It feeds measurement and routing and grants no trust. | immutable artifact by content hash: signed once per change and revision; a restamp is a new artifact and never replaces the stamp a review saw | jig:provenance-stamper | shared:ext.engineering-workflows, jig:protected-branch, s1.source-adapters |
| shared:ReviewerSeedCatch | One reviewer's seeded known-bad changes caught over those the reviewer received in a window, with its interval, computed by Ratchet's evaluation from the review outcomes and the seed frame. Never names which changes were seeds. Restricted to its one reader, the secure-creation measurement plane, which folds the count into that reviewer's own drift report; no reviewer, manager or other part receives it from Ratchet. | latest-wins record: keyed by reviewer and window; recomputed by rule from review outcomes and the seed frame, so a resend replaces the window's value; a window with no record reads as silent, never as zero | s7.road-adoption-telemetry | jig:measurement-plane |
| shared:SeededChange | A known-bad agent-written change the sampling service opens on a sandbox branch for the reviewer-drift baseline, never on the protected branch: the branch, the revision and the diff, and nothing else. It carries no fixture binding, no original or patched outcome and no test result, which stay in the sampling service's own frame, so the engineering system and the reviewer never receive what the change was built to show. Which changes were seeded stays in that frame. Drawn as it is, a seeded change does not pick up the triage labels, AI review comments, routing and provenance stamp a real change collects on its way through the protected branch, so a careful reviewer may tell it from a real one and the catch rate may read higher than the truth. | immutable artifact by content hash: keyed by sandbox branch and revision; a reseed is a new branch and never replaces a change a reviewer has seen | s7.sampling-service | shared:ext.engineering-workflows, jig:people.change-reviewer, jig:people.high-risk-reviewer |
| shared:DerivedCheckMisses | How often changes get past one derived check while its weakness stays: the replay failures of the check's kept test on changes deployed since the check's version was issued, over a window, against the floor set for the check. Counted by the regression catalog from the replay verdicts it receives, and this count is all of them that reaches the loop: no change id, no replay result and no test content. The pipeline stage still shows a deploy's pass or fail, as it does without the loop. The loop shows the count beside its own log of checks dropped or disabled, and a check over its floor goes to the policy-owning team, who can retire it from the check suite. The security risk owner sets each check's floor in the dial register. A request back to Ratchet to rework a check is not drawn in this edition; retiring is the owner's way out. | latest-wins record: keyed by check id, check version and window; recomputed by rule from replay results, so a resend replaces the window's value; a window with no record reads as silent, never as zero | s6.regression-catalog | jig:measurement-plane, jig:people.creation-policy-owner |
| shared:ClassTestTotals | Per-class counts delivered to the policy-owning team: people, never an agent. Carries rule and class versions, members tested, caught and not run. Only the regression catalog produces this projection, and it delivers it directly to that team as people, never an agent. It never reaches an agent-reachable part, the check suite, the measurement plane or the model route; the owner keeps it out of agent-readable rules, guidance and reports. It carries no finding identity, revision, location or test output. | versioned record: rule and class versions; delivered directly to the policy-owning team: people, never an agent, with no agent-readable copy | s6.regression-catalog | jig:people.creation-policy-owner |
| shared:ChangeRecord | Accountable owner, reviewed correction, release or policy revision, deployed state, valid retest; the SLSA build level of the provenance behind the deployed state, and a self-attested label when it is below Build Level 2. | at-least-once: keyed by finding id plus change id | s6.owner-change-connector, s6.hardening-service | s6.change-closure-records, s6.release-state-correlator, s6.retest-regression-planner, s6.enterprise-adapters, shared:ext.engineering-workflows, s5.failure-class-registry, s6.paved-road-catalog, s7.value-ledger, s5.disposition-review |
| shared:GateFailurePosture | Gate id, protected finding and path references, posture (fail-closed \| fail-open-with-record \| fail-open-under-expiring-acceptance), basis (max consequence tier reached, whether any reached asset was unattested and the unattested_default applied, optional impact_score, blocking-cost tier, threat signals), declared dependency scope (artifact, policy and identity references; deploys outside it proceed and are recorded while the gate is unavailable), threshold version and security risk owner, decided-at, expiry (from the posture_expiry_hours dial), tier default to apply if the posture expires or its key is revoked, signature and key reference, override reference (business risk owner acceptance id, security risk owner concurrence id, expiry) or null. Never carries a model exploitability estimate. | signed artifact with expiry: verified offline by the pipeline stage; an expired or untrusted posture falls back to the tier default it carries | s3.gate-failure-policy | s3.policy-decision-point, s6.enterprise-adapters, shared:ext.engineering-workflows |
| shared:ToolLead | A lead from a tool, a reviewer or an outside report. Labeled as a lead: never a fact, never coverage evidence, never a disposition. Leads arrive before the source loop's own triage, so its verdict is a label and never a filter. A rule-summary lead stands for a high-volume rule instead of one lead per hit, with a sample of hits drawn at random, each a hit-form lead. Its outsider-writable fields are released only to the quarantined lead reader and, as outsider text, with a lead the developer-queue filter routes to a team's queue; never to producer context. Only Ratchet's parts write it; the tool signal intake normalizes into it the secure-creation loop's leads, which arrive as the loop's own lead record. | at-least-once: keyed by tool, rule id (with rename mapping) and fingerprint; status updates append | s1.tool-signal-intake | s1.context-compiler, s5.evidence-intake-validator, s7.tool-yield-evaluator, s6.developer-queue-filter, s2.quarantined-lead-reader, s1.tool-lead-store, shared:ext.engineering-workflows |
| shared:DeliveryTelemetry | Per tool gate and service: stage time, queue time, deploys blocked or delayed, triage time per lead, from pipeline and tracker data the profile lists as collected. | at-least-once: keyed by tool gate, service and period | shared:ext.engineering-workflows | s7.tool-delivery-impact |
| shared:ChangeProvenance | Provenance for one change, recorded as a declared fact, never an observed one. The engineering system records every change, labeled or not, with its review outcomes and, when the change carries a provenance stamp, a reference to it. The source adapters check the stamp's signature against keys the organization registers, set the verification status, and fill the person, tool, model, session and skill-bundle hash only from the verified stamp, so measurement never reads a declared fact the signature did not cover. The author of an agent-generated change is the agent together with the person whose session produced it. A change with no stamp counts as unlabeled, and a stamp that cannot be verified counts as unverified; both stay in every denominator, never dropped or assigned an origin. | at-least-once: keyed by change id and revision; a later attestation verification appends, never overwrites; an unlabeled change is recorded as unlabeled and an unverifiable stamp as unverified | shared:ext.engineering-workflows | s1.source-adapters, s1.source-facts, s7.road-adoption-telemetry |
| shared:TrackerText | Issue and review text from the organization's engineering system: an issue, a comment on one, a change's description or a review comment, with the ids that place it. Anyone the tracker lets write, people outside the organization included, can write the title and the body, so both are marked untrusted. Restricted to its one reader, the quarantined reader: a coding agent never reads it, and receives only the reader's typed answer through the reader's schema gate. | at-least-once: keyed by item id and the time the text was written; an edited issue or comment appends a new record, and only the quarantined reader receives it | shared:ext.engineering-workflows | jig:quarantined-reader |

<a id="r2-typed-fields"></a>
## Contracts with typed fields

These contracts declare each field with its type, so the two sides of an interface build and validate against the same shape; the kit ships them as one JSON Schema. A field marked outsider-written never reaches a part where coding agents run.

### ClassTestResult

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rule_id` | id | yes |  |
| `rule_version` | string | yes |  |
| `proven_class_id` | id | yes |  |
| `proven_class_version` | integer | yes |  |
| `tested_on` | date | yes |  |
| `members` | list of object | yes | One per reproduced member of the class |
| `members.finding_id` | id | yes |  |
| `members.revision` | string | yes | The member's revision before the fix |
| `members.outcome` | one of caught, missed, not-run | yes |  |
| `members.location` | string | no | Where the rule fired on the member |
| `members.rule_output` | text | no | What the rule reported on the member |
| `members_tested` | integer | yes |  |
| `members_caught` | integer | yes |  |
| `members_not_run` | integer | yes |  |

| `tool_version` | string | no |  |

### shared:TrackerText

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `item_id` | id | yes | The issue, comment, change description or review comment the text belongs to |
| `item_kind` | one of issue, issue-comment, change-description, review-comment | yes |  |
| `change_id` | id | no | The change a change description or review comment belongs to |
| `written_at` | date-time | yes |  |
| `title` | text | no | outsider-written |
| `body` | text | yes | outsider-written |

<a id="r2-how-one-exact-action-is-authorized"></a>
## How one exact action is authorized

1. `s2.producer-adapters` to `s3.action-broker`: Receive typed proposal: S2 worker → S3 broker. No embedded credential or authority.
2. `s3.action-broker` to `s3.policy-decision-point`: Normalize and recheck: actual destination, current target, prerequisites, policy and approval.
3. `s3.policy-decision-point` to `s3.grant-approval-store`: Persist decision and grant: denial or exact expiring binding; mandatory audit before effect.
4. `s3.policy-decision-point` to `s3.local-enforcement-point`: Enforce at the runner: atomically consume applicable approval; check identity, limits and health. Only the authorized request proceeds.
5. `s3.local-enforcement-point` to `s3.bounded-runner`: Execute within the cell: scoped secret lease; observed outcome; cleanup, stop and incident rules.
6. `s3.bounded-runner` to `s5.independent-verifier`: Independently verify: receipt + protected oracle + telemetry; confirm / reject / unresolved.

<a id="r2-relationships"></a>
## Relationships (524)

Every relationship between parts. Content marks what flows: untrusted text from outsiders, or structured fields. The model-boundary rules are defined over these. Cleared by names, for a structured relationship out of a part exposed to outsider text, the person or model-free part on the relationship that clears the outsider label, and what its check does; fields it does not clear keep the label.

<a id="r2-every-part-by-plane-trust-zone"></a>
## Every part by plane, trust zone and quality tier

FIG-PART-MAP, the full part map, for agents: too dense to read as an image, so read it as structure. Each part sits in its plane and trust zone, and its fill shows its quality tier: control safety (T0), evidence integrity (T1), orchestration (T2), best effort and analytics (T3), or an existing system outside Ratchet (EXT). Control parts block when down; evidence and orchestration queue and retry; analytics continue without. A thick dashed border marks a swap point, a fine dashed one an existing tool.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  %% FIG-PART-MAP: Every part by plane, trust zone and quality tier
  %% Node ids are the model's element ids; each component is folded into its nearest ancestor in the same plane. Subgraphs are planes (plane-<plane>), trust zones inside them (zone-<plane>-<zone>) and people (people-roles).
  subgraph plane-integration["Integration (existing enterprise tools)"]
    subgraph zone-integration-reasoning["trust zone: Reasoning and workflow boundary"]
```
